## Batch Onboarding Wave 1 (2026-08-30): 10 Companies Added in Parallel

Followed up on the audit above by building the 10 easiest-to-classify gaps in parallel (each as an independent agent, briefed to read this playbook first): **Groww, Razorpay, CRED, Meesho, Paytm** (Greenhouse/Lever — confirmed via public documented REST APIs), **eClerx** (Oracle HCM CE — a third known example of this ATS, alongside Hexaware/Chubb/Icertis), and **Macquarie, MUFG, ING, SAP Labs** (Workday ×3, SuccessFactors — patterns this repo already had ~15 solved examples for). Registry now covers **84 companies**. See the "Current Companies" table above for per-company ATS/fetch-method rows.

**Toptal, Turing, and Trilogy were explicitly skipped** — recon found Toptal/Turing expose only their own tiny corporate-HQ hiring boards (e.g. "CEO, AI Services"), not the freelance-marketplace gig listings the original "(Remote)" framing implied, and neither that nor Trilogy's unusual apply-and-screen funnel fits this watcher's "new job posting" alert model at all. Decision: skip all three rather than build something technically present but functionally wrong. If freelance/gig-placement alerts are ever wanted, that needs a different data source and probably a different notification model, not a `fetch_jobs()`-shaped fetcher.

**Coordination approach for future waves:** each agent was told to build ONLY its own new files (`src/<slug>_fetcher.py`, `seen_jobs_<slug>.json`) and to report back — not apply — the exact `_PIPELINE_DATA` row, capability-set memberships, and `config.yaml` block. All shared-file edits (`company_registry.py`, `config.yaml`, `PLAYBOOK.md`, `README.md`) were then merged centrally by hand afterward. This avoided any concurrent-edit conflicts across 10 simultaneous agents touching the same files, and let one person sanity-check consistency (alphabetical ordering, correct capability-set membership, no duplicate slugs) before merging. **Reuse this pattern for future batches** — do not let parallel company-onboarding agents edit shared files directly.

**New lessons from this wave** (beyond the per-company notes already folded into the "Current Companies" table above):
- **Real-world validation of the two-track design:** SAP Labs' live data included an actual "Senior/Staff AI Engineer" posting in Bangalore mentioning LangGraph — the first live posting anywhere in this repo confirmed to match via the new `AI / ML / Python` track rather than `.NET / C#`. The dual-track matching isn't just theoretical.
- **Greenhouse boards can double-encode HTML entities.** Both Groww and Razorpay's `content` field came back as `&amp;lt;div&amp;gt;` (entity-encoded twice) rather than plain `&lt;div&gt;` — the repo's usual `strip-tags-then-unescape` order leaves visible tag text in that case. Unescape first, then strip tags (safe either way).
- **Lever boards often have no "India" substring in `categories.location`** (bare "Bangalore, Karnataka" style strings) — CRED, Meesho, and Paytm all needed the posting's own structured `country` field (`"IN"`) instead of a city-name guess or the plain-substring `is_india_job()` check. When integrating a new Lever-based company, check for this field before assuming location text alone is enough.
- **A company name is not proof you found the right ATS tenant.** Macquarie's own `mq.wd3.myworkdayjobs.com/CareersatMQ` Workday tenant returns real job data — for **Macquarie University** (Sydney), an unrelated organization sharing the name. Always confirm the organization/branding visible in the actual response, not just that a plausible-looking URL returns HTTP 200 with job-shaped JSON.
- **Workday pagination can silently wrap around instead of terminating.** MUFG's tenant returns the identical page-1 job IDs when `offset >= total`, rather than an empty list (a new variant of the UBS BrassRing wraparound bug already in this table) — detect via a repeated-first-ID memo, not just an empty-batch check.
- **A confirmed-zero-India-jobs result can be entirely correct.** ING (0 of 734 global jobs in India) and eClerx (0 India titles pass `title_family` — its India postings are 100% BPO/KPO ops roles) are both mechanically verified working fetchers with a genuine current zero, not bugs. Don't assume "0 matches" means "broken" without checking the raw fetched data first — the Hexaware/Persistent lesson from the audit above generalizes here too.

**A pre-existing bug surfaced by MUFG's real title data, FIXED 2026-08-31 after repeated evidence:** `matching.exclude_terms` was matched via the same non-word-boundary substring check as everything else in `matcher.py`. Two of the existing short exclude terms were dangerous: **`"VP"` is a substring of `"AVP"`/`"SVP"`/`"EVP"`** — extremely common banking title tiers across this repo's ~40 BFSI companies — and **`"director"` is a substring of `"Active Directory"`**. Originally flagged (not fixed) when MUFG's real live data hit both (`"Full Stack Developer - AVP"`, `"Active Directory Senior Engineer"` incorrectly excluded); the deliberate-decision threshold was reached once the same pattern independently cost real matches at **Moody's** (`"AVP-Systems Engineer"` excluded) and **BlackRock** (the majority of India engineering postings carry `"Vice President"`/`"Director"` banking seniority bands that are, in BlackRock's structure, still genuine IC roles — not people-management titles).

**Fix:** `matcher.py` now has `_contains_any_whole_word()` (regex `\bterm\b` after the same `_normalize_text` normalization), and `passes_exclude_check()` uses it instead of the plain-substring `_contains_any()`. **This applies only to `exclude_terms`** — `title_family`/`skills`/`primary_skills` still use plain substring matching deliberately (e.g. `"SDE"` must still match inside `"SDE2"` — there's a dedicated test for it; word-boundary matching would break that). Verified safe: every existing `exclude_terms` entry is either a multi-word phrase (space-delimited phrases were never at risk — the bug was specific to short single-word terms with no boundary against an adjacent word) or a single word that was always intended to match as a whole word, never as a fragment. Regression tests added: `AVP`/`SVP`/`EVP`/`Active Directory` no longer excluded; bare `VP`/`Director` titles still correctly excluded.

**Remaining gap after this wave: 21 companies** (34 originally missing, minus these 10, minus Toptal/Turing/Trilogy skipped): BNP Paribas, CRISIL, Amdocs, CitiusTech, Mastek, Mphasis, Jio Platforms, PhonePe, Zomato, Swiggy, Dream11, Nykaa, Zerodha, ShareChat/Moj, PolicyBazaar, Lenskart, Google, Meta, Flipkart, Atlassian, Intuit, NEC Software Solutions. Recon on these ran out of budget before confirming most of their ATS platforms (see the earlier audit's classification) — Google and Meta are confirmed hard (no public API, heavy client-side rendering); the rest need the standard Step-1 ATS identification before assignment.

## Batch Onboarding Wave 2 (2026-08-31): 21 More Companies Attempted

Finished the rest of the 65-company target list from the audit above, plus completed the "Toptal/Turing" split. **20 of 21 succeeded** (NEC Software Solutions, Lenskart, Mphasis, PhonePe, Amdocs, CitiusTech, Mastek, CRISIL, BNP Paribas, ShareChat, PolicyBazaar, Meta, Google, Nykaa, Zomato, Jio Platforms, Flipkart, Swiggy, Zerodha, Atlassian, Intuit — see "Current Companies" table above). Registry now covers **105 companies**. Only **Dream11** came back genuinely infeasible: its Lever board (`jobs.lever.co/dreamsports`) worked as recently as Feb 2026 (confirmed via Wayback Machine) but 404s today from two independent network paths; current postings live entirely on a third-party staffing marketplace (Uplers) that doesn't fit this repo's per-company fetcher model. No fetcher was built for it — worth rechecking later in case Lever comes back, or as a dedicated Uplers investigation.

**Two "confirmed hard" companies from the prior audit turned out feasible** — both Google and Meta were flagged hard on 2026-08-30 based on "no public API exists." Both actually have an anonymous, unauthenticated path once you look past the officially-deprecated public API: Google server-renders results into a JSON blob in the page HTML, Meta exposes an anonymous internal GraphQL endpoint. **Lesson: "no public API" and "no discoverable API" are different claims** — always do the live SSR/network-capture check yourself rather than trusting a prior negative assessment, even one from this same playbook.

**Rate-limit lesson learned mid-wave:** the first attempt at this wave launched 20 companies in one fully-parallel batch and immediately exhausted the session's rate limit — every agent failed with HTTP 429 within seconds of each other, and none of the 429'd agents were resumable afterward (they'd already fully exited). **Fix: batch parallel company-onboarding agents in groups of 5-7, not 20+**, and pass along any partial findings a failed agent's last message revealed (an ATS name, a confirmed endpoint, a token) into the retry prompt so the redo isn't from-scratch. Several agents that got cut off mid-report (not mid-failure — e.g. NEC, Lenskart, Mphasis, Flipkart, MUFG in wave 1) had actually already written complete, working fetcher files before the cutoff; always check disk for stray files and test them directly before assuming a "no final report" agent produced nothing.

**Four new ATS vendors discovered this wave**, none previously in this repo: **TurboHire** (Flipkart — anonymous `token/noauth` bearer-JWT flow), **MyNextHire** (Swiggy, then reused directly for ShareChat — no keyword/pagination params, full JD inline), **Skima AI** (Nykaa — Astro SSR career-page builder), **Darwinbox** (Zomato — validated against sibling tenants Zepto/Hetero since Zomato's own tenant is empty), **Trakstar Hire** (PolicyBazaar — legacy RecruiterBox rebrand, RSS-feed based). RippleHire (already known from LTIMindtree) got two more integrations (Mphasis, CitiusTech); SmartRecruiters (already known from Nagarro) got two more (NEC, PhonePe).

**Two more pre-existing config gaps found, NOT fixed in this wave** (same category as the VP/director substring bug from wave 1 — flagged for a deliberate decision, not bundled in silently):
- **`title_family` doesn't cover "Lead Engineer - I/II" / "Technical Specialist - II" style leveling.** CitiusTech's and several IT-services companies' genuinely tech-signal-rich postings (confirmed via live description fetch — real .NET, real LangChain/Gen-AI work) never reach the skill-matching stage because the title itself doesn't match any phrase in the shared `title_family` list. This is a real, current, silent loss of legitimate matches — the fix is additive (new title phrases) but is a global `config.yaml` change affecting all 105 companies and should be reviewed deliberately.
- **`exclude_locations`'s "Kochi" doesn't catch "Cochin"** (the same city, alternate spelling) or other Kerala cities some companies use (Ernakulam, Thrissur, Kottayam) — found via Jio Platforms' live location data. Same substring-matching class of gap as VP/director; same reasoning for not fixing it reflexively here.

**Company coverage status vs. the original 65-name target list:** 61 of 65 are now registered (all except Toptal, Turing, Trilogy — deliberately skipped — and Dream11 — currently infeasible). The user's follow-up request added a much larger second list (100+ additional BFSI/GCC, insurance, consultancy, and AI-native companies) as a further expansion beyond the original 65.

## Batch Onboarding Wave 3 (2026-08-31): First 15 From the Expanded List + Full System Health Check

Started the user's expanded list (100+ companies beyond the original 65), prioritizing BFSI/GCC .NET+AI shops and AI-native product companies first per their explicit ask. **15 companies added**, registry now covers **120 companies**: DTCC, Arcesium, Nasdaq, ICE, Moody's, Bloomberg, Swiss Re, AIG (financial-services/GCC tier); ANZ, Lloyds Banking Group, Visa, BlackRock, NatWest Group (more BFSI GCCs); UiPath, AlphaSense (AI-native product companies). Two more new ATS vendors discovered: **Ashby** (UiPath) and **Talemetry** (NatWest — sits behind a Cloudflare managed challenge that blocks Firefox indefinitely but passes cleanly under Chromium with `--disable-blink-features=AutomationControlled` and an enforced ~5s gap between requests, the opposite lesson from Honeywell/IBM/Tech Mahindra).

**Real matches confirmed across both tracks on this batch alone**: ICE (`.NET / C#`, explicit C#/.NET Core/ASP.NET Core mention), Moody's (9 matches, including one genuinely hybrid `.NET / C#` + `AI / ML / Python` role), AlphaSense (`AI / ML / Python`, explicit LangChain/Langfuse/prompt-engineering mention — the "RAG over financial documents" narrative fit called out when this company was picked), UiPath (`.NET / C#`, .NET Core/Angular/Azure), BlackRock (`AI / ML / Python`, explicit LangChain/generative-ai mention). AIG, Swiss Re, ANZ, Nasdaq currently show a genuine 0 — real facts about small/specific India offices right now, not bugs (same class as ING/eClerx).

**Rate limit hit repeatedly again even at 6-7 concurrent agents** (not just the 20-agent batch from Wave 2) — the session's rate-limit budget was simply more depleted by this point in a long, heavy day. Lesson reinforced: when a batch partially fails, check disk for salvageable files before relaunching (DTCC, Arcesium, Lloyds, Visa, BlackRock, and NatWest all left complete, working files despite their agent's final status showing "failed" or an unfinished-sounding last message) — relaunch fresh only for the slugs that are actually missing.

**Full-registry health check performed** (all 120 companies, one process, `fetch_jobs()` called directly with a 30s-per-company cutoff, zero side effects): 112 immediately OK, 6 flagged — all 6 investigated individually in isolated fresh processes:
- **Honeywell, Tech Mahindra** — flagged here as false alarms at the time ("both work perfectly in a fresh process... not of production"). **This conclusion was wrong — see the Wave 4 entry below, where the same error was found happening in real production runs and fixed properly.**
- **HCLTech, Wipro** — false alarms from an overly-tight 30s cutoff. Both are legitimately large-pool companies (Wipro: 2607 jobs, confirmed fetched successfully in 229s in isolation; HCLTech has an even larger configured `max_listings: 8500`). Production has no such timeout — these just take longer per cycle, which is fine.
- **Persistent** — genuine, confirmed-ongoing external outage at Zwayam's API (`apipersistent.zwayam.com`, HTTP 503 / connection failure via raw `curl`, independent of this repo's code) — the exact same outage flagged in the original 2026-08-30 Company Coverage Audit, now into its second day. Not a code bug.
- **Virtusa — a real bug, found and fixed.** Taleo's search API was returning `{"requisitionList": null, ..., "careerSectionUnAvailable": true}` (HTTP 200, not an error status) instead of an empty result — the fetcher's `.get("requisitionList", [])` never falls back to `[]` because the key is present with value `None`, not absent, so it crashed with `TypeError: 'NoneType' object is not iterable`. Fixed: check `careerSectionUnAvailable` explicitly and treat it as a transient empty result; `.get("requisitionList") or []` as a second layer of defense. This is a genuinely new failure mode worth remembering — a "success" HTTP status with an explicit unavailability flag and null payload, not a 5xx.

**The `exclude_terms` VP/director substring bug (flagged, not fixed, in Wave 1) was fixed this wave.** Originally left alone as a judgment call since it had only been observed once (MUFG). By this wave it had independently cost real matches at Moody's (`"AVP-Systems Engineer"`) and the *majority* of BlackRock's India matches (BlackRock's internal titling puts "Vice President"/"Director" on genuine IC roles, not just people-managers) — three independent companies hitting the same false-positive class was enough evidence to justify a real fix rather than another flag. Added `_contains_any_whole_word()` to `matcher.py` (word-boundary regex matching) and switched `passes_exclude_check()` to use it — scoped to `exclude_terms` only; `title_family`/`skills`/`primary_skills` deliberately keep plain substring matching (the `"SDE"` inside `"SDE2"` design still needs it, verified via the existing dedicated test). 6 new regression tests added (`AVP`/`SVP`/`EVP`/`Active Directory` no longer excluded; bare `VP`/`Director` still are). All 159 tests pass.

**As of this entry: 98 files changed, 44 new fetcher modules, nothing yet committed or pushed** — the live production GitHub Actions workflow is still running the pre-session state (74 companies, `.NET/C#`-only matching, no tags). See the commit/deploy status note the user asked for directly in the session transcript, not duplicated here.

## Batch Onboarding Wave 4 (2026-09-01): 11 More Companies + Production False-Positive Investigation

**11 companies added, registry now covers 131 companies**: Zeta, Juspay, M2P Fintech, Yubi, Signzy (Lever-family fintech, cache-once, `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS` + `_INLINE_DESCRIPTIONS`), Automation Anywhere, Thomson Reuters (Workday, neither flag), ServiceNow (`_IGNORES_KEYWORDS` + `_SUPPORTS_LOCATION`), DataRobot (`_IGNORES_KEYWORDS` only), Glean (`_IGNORES_KEYWORDS` + `_INLINE_DESCRIPTIONS`), and **Perfios** (new ATS: Darwinbox `candidatev2`, the newer Cloudflare-gated SPA front-end — see below).

**New ATS lesson — Darwinbox `candidatev2` rejects a same-session manual re-fetch of its own search API.** `perfios.darwinbox.in`'s `GET /ms/candidateapi/job/alljobs` call, replayed via `page.evaluate(fetch(url))` after page load (the pattern used successfully elsewhere, e.g. BNP Paribas), consistently returned HTTP 422 (`"no job found with that id"`) — even though a live network capture proved the *exact same URL* succeeds when the SPA calls it itself during natural page load. Whatever makes the request valid (a short-lived token attached by an earlier `getCompanyConfig` call, or simply request timing/ordering) isn't reproduced by a manual same-session re-fetch. **Fix: attach a `page.on("response", ...)` listener before navigating, and capture the SPA's own request/response instead of replaying it** — sidesteps the problem entirely rather than trying to reverse-engineer it. Keep the old manual-fetch as a fallback in case the listener ever misses the request (route change, timing). This is a new, reusable pattern distinct from the existing Societe Generale lesson (which found an unauthenticated server-rendered alternative) — here there was no alternative endpoint, so capturing the SPA's own traffic was the only way through.

Perfios's board is tiny (3 open postings, all Customer Success — zero engineering right now, same "confirmed-zero-is-a-real-fact" class as ING/eClerx). One posting's `jd` field is genuinely `<p><br></p>` (empty) in the API response itself — not a fetcher bug, just a posting with no JD content; the other two have real HTML bodies. None of the 3 would pass Layer 2 (title_family) even if they had rich descriptions, so this has zero effect on real alerts.

### Production false-positive investigation (user-reported, high priority)

User reported three symptoms from a specific evening: no AI/ML matches at all, all `.NET/C#` matches arriving in one burst, and a "Java developer" match at Citi. Investigated using live GitHub Actions logs (`gh run list` / `gh run view --log`) and the exact `seen_jobs_citi.json` commit diff to identify the real job IDs involved, rather than guessing:

1. **"No AI/ML matches" — expected, not a bug.** The complained-about run (`33403221029`, started 2026-08-31 14:32 UTC = 20:02 IST, i.e. "around 8:30pm") ran on the commit **before** the AI/ML track was deployed (`e61038d`/`07e19eb` weren't pushed until 16:50 UTC / 22:20 IST that night). The old code had no AI/ML `title_family`, `skills`, or `primary_skills` terms at all — it structurally could not produce an AI/ML match. Confirmed the very next run after deploy (`33436040797`) did send real AI/ML-tagged matches (AlphaSense, BlackRock, etc., cross-checked in the Wave 3 entry above).

2. **"Java developer at Citi" — a real match, working as designed, not a bug.** Traced via `seen_jobs_citi.json`'s diff in commit `5c848ba` to the exact job: ID `26990632`, titled *"FI Risk - Core Java Backend Developer"*. Its live JD states verbatim: *"The skills required for this job include: Java (or .NET Core), Microservices, APIs, Kafka, Flink, and ECS."* — Citi itself is telling candidates .NET Core is an accepted alternative to Java for this role. This is the identical pattern already documented for ICE's "Java (or .NET Core)" posting in Wave 3 and treated as a genuine fit then, not a bug — Shivangi is a real candidate for a role that explicitly accepts her stack, even though the title alone reads as Java-only. Flagged to the user as a judgment call (tighten `require_tech_in_description` to reject "or .NET"-style secondary mentions vs. keep catching these) rather than silently changing it.

   Separately (found by the matcher, not this specific complaint's job): Citi job `26960320`, *"Senior Python Application Developer"*, **was** a real bug — wrongly tagged `[.NET / C#]` purely because its JD named "SQL Server" as its database. Already root-caused and fixed earlier this session (see `config.yaml`'s `primary_skills` comment, dated 2026-08-31) by removing `"SQL Server"`/`"T-SQL"`/`"Web API"` from the `.NET / C#` hard-skill group — they stay in the broader soft `skills` list only. This fix was sitting uncommitted; shipped in this wave.

3. **"All matches bunched together" — a real, pre-existing, worsening infrastructure issue, now mitigated.** `watcher.yml` schedules every 30 min with `concurrency: {group: job-watcher, cancel-in-progress: false}` — a queued trigger waits for the in-progress run to finish rather than running concurrently. Checked actual run history (`gh run list`): runs have been taking **35–70 minutes** end-to-end (not the ~30 min the cron interval assumes) since at least 2026-08-28, well before this session's company expansion — producing real gaps of 2–8 hours between deliveries, not a steady 30-min cadence. Every job discovered across one of these multi-hour gaps arrives in a single alert burst at the end of it, which is exactly what was reported. This was already happening before Wave 4, and doubling the roster (74→131 companies this session) will only make per-run time worse if left alone. **Mitigation shipped**: bumped `run_all.py --workers` from 10 to 20 in `watcher.yml` — only 8 of 131 fetchers use Playwright (heavy/stateful), the rest are plain HTTP, so higher thread concurrency should meaningfully cut wall-clock time back toward the intended 30-min cadence without meaningfully increasing runner resource pressure. Not fully re-verified against a live scheduled run yet (that requires waiting for the next few cron firings) — worth checking `gh run list` again in a few hours to confirm run duration actually dropped.

All 160 tests pass; `run_all.py --validate` confirms clean wiring for all 131 companies.

### Critical fix: Playwright-based fetchers have been silently broken in every production run since at least 2026-08-28

While investigating the "bunched alerts" complaint above, `gh run view --log` on the live triggered-verification run (post-Wave-4 deploy) showed **every one of the 8 Playwright-based companies fetching 0 jobs**, each logging `RateLimitError: ... cache fill failed: It looks like you are using Playwright Sync API inside the asyncio loop.` Checked historical runs going back to 2026-08-28 (before this session's changes even started) — **100% of scheduled runs show this same failure for these companies.** The Wave 3 entry above dismissing this exact error as "a health-check-script artifact, not a production issue" was wrong: it never actually checked a real scheduled `run_all.py` production run's log, only an isolated fresh-process re-test.

**Root cause, confirmed empirically (not just theorized) via local reproduction**: `run_all.py` submits every company to one shared `ThreadPoolExecutor`, which reuses a fixed set of OS threads across the full company list as tasks complete. Each of the 8 Playwright-based fetchers (`bnpparibas`, `honeywell`, `ibm`, `natwest`, `perfios`, `servicenow`, `techmahindra`, `virtusa`) keeps its browser open as a **module-level singleton for the whole process's life** (`atexit`-only cleanup — deliberate, to avoid relaunching Firefox on every call). The first time any one of these modules calls `sync_playwright().start()` on a given OS thread, it succeeds and binds an asyncio event loop to that thread for good. If the pool later reuses that *same* thread for a *different* Playwright-based company's `_ensure_browser()` call, Playwright's sync API sees the leftover loop and refuses outright.

Reproduced directly: running all 8 real fetchers' `fetch_jobs()` concurrently with `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=4)` (one thread per company, no reuse) — **0 failures**. Running the same 8 through `max_workers=3` (forcing at least 2-3 threads to each handle multiple different Playwright companies) — **3/8 failed** with the exact production error. Running all 8 through `max_workers=8` (one dedicated thread per company again) — **0 failures, every company returned its real job count** (BNP Paribas 616, IBM 665, Honeywell 200, Tech Mahindra 76, NatWest 56, ServiceNow 39, Perfios 3). This conclusively isolates the trigger to *thread reuse across two different Playwright-based modules*, not "any two Playwright instances alive at once" (they can run fully concurrently on separate threads with zero issue).

**Fix**: `company_registry.py` gets a new `uses_playwright: bool` field on `CompanyPipeline` and a `_USES_PLAYWRIGHT` frozenset (the 8 slugs above). `run_all.py::run_companies()` now splits the company list into two groups and opens two separate `ThreadPoolExecutor`s: the general pool (still sized by `--workers`) for everyone else, and a **dedicated pool sized 1:1 with however many Playwright-based companies are selected**, guaranteeing every one of them gets its own OS thread for the entire run and never shares it with another Playwright-based module. Both pools run concurrently with each other. Verified end-to-end via the real `run_all.py` CLI (not just direct fetcher calls) — all 8 companies returned `ok` with real, substantial fetch counts and zero asyncio-loop errors in the full log.

This means **Honeywell, Tech Mahindra, Virtusa, BNP Paribas, and NatWest have likely never sent Shivangi a single real alert since being added** — their entire pipeline was silently returning 0 jobs on every single scheduled run, invisibly (no pipeline failure — a clean 0-job "success" looks identical to a genuinely quiet company in the logs, which is exactly why this stayed hidden). Perfios and ServiceNow (added this wave) would have had the identical problem from their very first run had this not been caught during the post-deploy live-verification check. This is a strong argument for periodically re-running the full health check against **real scheduled-run logs**, not just isolated fresh-process re-tests — the two environments do not behave the same way once thread-pool reuse is involved.

Lesson for future Playwright-based fetchers: any new one must be added to `_USES_PLAYWRIGHT` in `company_registry.py` (there's now a dedicated registry test, `test_registry_flags_playwright_backed_fetchers`, enforcing the exact expected set) — forgetting this silently reintroduces the exact same bug for that company.

**Correction, same day: the dedicated-thread-per-company fix above was not sufficient.** Deployed it, triggered a live verification run (`33476619607`), and 7 of the 8 companies *still* failed with the identical "asyncio loop" error — all within a ~3 second window right at the start of the run, meaning they were genuinely racing each other on their (now-guaranteed-separate) dedicated threads. This never reproduced locally on a multi-core Mac; it reproduced reliably on GitHub Actions' CPU-constrained runner. Conclusion: thread reuse was one real trigger (fixed, keep it), but simultaneous `sync_playwright().start()` calls from different threads racing under CPU contention is a *second*, independent trigger the local repro never exercised.

**Real fix**: added `src/_playwright_startup.py`, a single module-level `threading.Lock()` (`STARTUP_LOCK`). Every one of the 8 Playwright-based fetchers now wraps only its one-time `sync_playwright().start()` + `.launch(...)` call in `with STARTUP_LOCK:` — serializing just the brief startup moment process-wide, while the actual per-company scraping that follows (page navigation, extraction) still runs fully concurrently across companies, since the lock is released immediately after launch. Guarded with a new test (`tests/test_playwright_startup_lock.py`) that AST-parses each Playwright-backed fetcher and confirms its `sync_playwright().start()` call is genuinely inside a `with STARTUP_LOCK:` block, not just that the string appears somewhere in the file — so a future Playwright-based fetcher that forgets to hold the lock around its actual launch call fails CI immediately instead of silently reproducing this bug.

**Lesson**: for concurrency bugs, a clean local repro is necessary but not sufficient — always verify the actual fix against a real triggered production run before considering it closed. This is the second time this exact bug fooled a "confirmed fine in isolation" check (see the original, wrong Wave 3 entry above); the pattern to watch for is any Playwright/thread-safety issue where the failure is sensitive to CPU contention or scheduling timing, since a fast local machine can mask races a constrained CI runner exposes reliably.

**Correction #2, same day: the shared-lock fix above ALSO failed in production.** Deployed it, triggered a second live verification run (`33481734513`), and the exact same 7 companies failed again with the identical error, at nearly the same relative moment (~5 seconds into the run) as before the lock existed — meaning `STARTUP_LOCK` had no observable effect. Read Playwright's actual `PlaywrightContextManager.__enter__` source directly (`playwright/sync_api/_context_manager.py`) rather than continuing to guess: the check is `asyncio.get_running_loop()` (thread-local by design) followed by `self._loop.is_running()` on a freshly created loop — which should be structurally impossible to trip on a guaranteed-fresh, never-reused, lock-serialized OS thread. Since two independent, individually-reasoned fixes for two different theorized mechanisms both failed identically in the same real environment, the honest conclusion is the exact trigger on GitHub Actions' runner was never fully identified — the local machine's inability to reproduce this bug at all (not even without any fix) made every "local pass" a false signal of confidence.

**Real fix**: stopped trying to make threads safe for this and sidestepped the entire class of problem. `run_all.py` now runs each of the 8 Playwright-based companies in its own **subprocess** (`subprocess.run([sys.executable, "run_company.py", slug, "--config", ...])`) instead of a thread in any pool — a fresh OS process has no possible shared Python state with any other company's process (no shared threads, no shared module-level singletons, no shared event loop of any kind), which removes every mechanism that could cause this regardless of which one was actually responsible. `STARTUP_LOCK` was left in place (harmless, zero-cost with one thread per process) as defense in depth. General (non-Playwright) companies are unaffected — still run in-process via the shared thread pool, exactly as before.

**Lesson, stated more sharply this time**: when a bug cannot be reproduced locally at all, "it doesn't happen in my test" carries zero evidentiary weight for whether a fix works — only a real triggered production run is evidence, and even a clean one should be treated cautiously until it's been seen at least once. When two different fixes for two different theorized root causes both fail identically, stop trying to out-theorize the specific mechanism and reach for the strongest structural guarantee available (full process isolation) rather than a third narrower patch.

### Correction #3 — the actual root cause, found by finally reading the FIRST exception instead of only the LAST one

Subprocess isolation (attempt 3) *also* failed identically in production (run `33489814084`). At that point the "different Playwright instances collide" theory had failed three independent, structurally-sound fixes in a row — a strong signal the theory itself was wrong, not that a fourth patch was needed. Went back to the raw log and, instead of grepping for `"asyncio loop"` (which is what every previous pass had done), grepped for `"search fetch failed"` / `"rate-limited for"` **excluding** that string, to see what each company's *first* attempt actually failed with, before any retry:

- `NatWest`: `BrowserType.launch: Executable doesn't exist at .../chromium_headless_shell-1234/...` — completely different browser (Chromium, not Firefox!) and a completely different, much simpler cause: **the workflow's `playwright install firefox` step never installs Chromium at all**, and NatWest deliberately uses Chromium (its Cloudflare challenge blocks Firefox — see the Wave 3 entry). Not a race, not a cache issue — just a browser that was never installed, full stop, every single run since the day NatWest was added.
- `Honeywell`, `Tech Mahindra` (no internal retry — the raw error surfaces directly): `BrowserType.launch: Executable doesn't exist at .../firefox-1538/firefox/firefox` — the **exact same bug class already found and fixed the same day in the sibling `ai-job-watcher` repo**: `requirements.txt` had `playwright>=1.44` (unpinned), so `pip install` resolved whatever's newest (1.62.0) each run; the browser cache key only hashes `requirements.txt`'s *text*, which never changed, so a stale/wrong-build Firefox kept getting restored from cache instead of the build `1.62.0` actually needs.
- `BNP Paribas`, `Perfios`, `ServiceNow`, `Virtusa` (**do** have an internal `for attempt in range(3):` retry around browser startup): same underlying `firefox-1538` executable-missing error on attempt 1 — but it was **never visible in any log from any of the first three fix attempts**, because `_ensure_browser()`'s original code was:
  ```python
  if _browser is None:
      with STARTUP_LOCK:
          _pw = sync_playwright().start()   # succeeds -- returns a live instance
          _browser = _pw.firefox.launch(headless=True)   # fails: wrong build cached
      atexit.register(_shutdown_browser)
  ```
  When `.launch()` raises, `_pw` is left pointing at a **live, never-stopped** Playwright instance (its background dispatcher greenlet+loop keeps running — `_shutdown_browser` is only `atexit`-registered, and that registration line is never reached on failure). The caller's retry loop catches the exception and tries again on attempt 2, which calls `_ensure_browser()` again — `_browser` is still `None`, so it calls `sync_playwright().start()` **again, on the same thread, while attempt 1's own instance is still alive** — which is genuinely, correctly detected by Playwright as "Sync API inside the asyncio loop" (this really is the one situation that check exists to catch). The retry loop's `last_exc` variable then gets overwritten with this second, unrelated-looking exception, and the original `firefox-1538` error — the actual root cause — is silently discarded and never printed anywhere. **Three straight fix attempts chased the symptom (the masking exception) because the masking exception was the only one anyone had ever seen.**

**Real fix (this time addressing the actual cause)**:
1. `requirements.txt`: pinned `playwright==1.62.0` (same fix as `ai-job-watcher`) — any future version bump now changes the cache key too, so the cache can never silently drift out of sync with the installed package again.
2. `watcher.yml`: `playwright install firefox` → `playwright install firefox chromium` (NatWest needs Chromium and it was simply never being installed); replaced the old bare `if cache-hit != true` gate with a real verify step that actually **launches both browsers** via a one-line Python smoke test and reinstalls if that fails — catches a stale/wrong-build cache even if the pin is ever loosened again, same pattern just added to `ai-job-watcher`.
3. **The actual bug**: every one of the 8 `_ensure_browser()` implementations now wraps `.launch(...)` in `try/except`, and on failure calls `_pw.stop()` and resets `_pw = None` before re-raising — so a caller-level retry always starts from a clean slate instead of colliding with a leaked, still-running instance from the failed attempt. This is the fix that actually matters going forward: even if a browser launch fails for some *future*, unrelated reason (a transient runner resource blip, a real new Playwright/browser incompatibility), the retry will now fail with the **true, original error message**, not a confusing masked one — which is what should have made this whole investigation take 10 minutes instead of most of a day.

**Lesson, sharper still**: when investigating a retried operation's failure, always check what the *first* attempt's exception actually said, not just the exception that finally propagated out after all retries were exhausted — a retry loop that doesn't log each attempt, or a bug (like the leaked `_pw` here) that makes attempt 2's failure look completely different from attempt 1's, will actively mislead you toward fixing the wrong layer. Three fix attempts (thread isolation, a startup lock, full subprocess isolation) were all individually well-reasoned and individually correct answers to *a* problem — just not the one that was actually happening. The tell, in hindsight, was that all three kept failing identically no matter how thoroughly the "concurrent Playwright instances collide" theory was addressed; that should have been the cue to question the theory itself much sooner rather than escalate to a stronger version of the same fix.

## Batch Onboarding Wave 5 (2026-09-03): GCCs — 7 Companies Added

Prompted by the user noticing United Airlines hiring in India and asking why Global Capability Centres (GCCs) — in-house engineering arms of multinationals, distinct from both BFSI GCCs (already heavily covered: Goldman, Citi, Wells Fargo, Barclays, JPMorgan, etc.) and IT-services vendors (TCS/Infosys/Wipro) — were underrepresented in the registry. A research pass surveyed aviation, retail/CPG, non-bank tech-product, pharma, and manufacturing GCCs across Bengaluru/Hyderabad/Gurugram (Pune- and Chennai-only candidates were explicitly excluded per the user's direction, since Chennai is already in `exclude_locations` and Pune-only GCCs like John Deere/Mercedes-Benz R&D/VW Group Technology Solutions would produce zero addressable matches). **7 companies added, registry now covers 138**: United Airlines (India Knowledge Center), Nvidia, Shell, Pfizer, Boeing (BIETC), PepsiCo India GCC, Walmart Global Tech.

**Every agent was explicitly instructed to read this playbook in full before starting** (the user's direct requirement) — each onboarding prompt opened with a mandatory Step 0 pointing here, and every agent's final report confirmed doing so and cited specific prior lessons (Macquarie/Macquarie-University naming-collision check for Shell, the UBS/MUFG pagination-wraparound pattern reused for Nvidia/Pfizer/Walmart, the Micron/Verizon/Lowe's facet-leakage lesson informing Boeing's `?l=` distrust).

**Headline finding: pre-onboarding "ATS: custom" guesses from secondary research were wrong 4 times out of 7.** Initial recon (aggregators, generic company descriptions) labeled Boeing, PepsiCo, Walmart, and United Airlines as "custom" ATS. Live DevTools-based verification found real, already-known platforms in every case: Boeing → **TalentBrew** (same vendor as Optum), PepsiCo → **iCIMS/Jibe**, Walmart → **Workday**, United Airlines → **Phenom People** (same family as Morningstar, but easier — the dedicated India landing page embeds a JSON blob in server-rendered HTML with no `/widgets` API dance needed). Only Nvidia, Shell, and Pfizer's pre-flagged "Workday" guesses turned out correct — because those were sourced from an actual `*.myworkdayjobs.com` URL, not a vibe. Added as a new "Key Bugs" row: a URL-shape guess from research that never opened a Network tab should be treated as unverified, not as a working assumption.

**Real matches confirmed across both tracks**: 21 total real matches surfaced across the 7 companies — United Airlines (4, all `AI / ML / Python`, explicit LangChain/CrewAI/LLM mentions on Bengaluru "Senior AI Engineer - Cybersecurity" roles), Nvidia (3, all `AI / ML / Python`, one explicitly naming LangChain/LangGraph/RAG/vector databases), Shell (1, `.NET / C#`, explicit "Java and .Net" mention), Walmart (13 — 11 `AI / ML / Python` genuine GenAI/LLM-platform roles, 2 `.NET / C#`). Pfizer, Boeing, and PepsiCo each returned a **genuine current zero** — same "confirmed-zero-is-a-real-fact" class as ING/eClerx/AIG documented in earlier waves, not fetcher defects: Pfizer's India pool is manufacturing/QA-skewed with no current Hyderabad tech postings; Boeing India's board is genuinely tiny (2 jobs, both hardware roles); PepsiCo has real AI/ML-shaped postings in its data but they're filtered out *before* the skill check by pre-existing global config gaps (see below), not by anything company-specific.

**Two new bug-pattern *shapes* joined the existing pagination-wraparound family** (UBS/MUFG/Nvidia/Pfizer/Walmart all "silently replay page 1 forever" once `offset >= total`):
- **PepsiCo's `offset` is ignored outright — it never advances even once**, a strictly worse variant than wraparound (verified via identical results at offset 0/5/20/50/100/200); worked around with the established cache-once-per-keyword pattern already used for UBS/Deutsche Bank/Persistent.
- **Shell's `limit > 20` silently no-ops** (empty result, no `total`, HTTP 200) rather than the clean HTTP 400 Northern Trust's tenant returns for the same over-cap condition — a failure shape that would look identical to "genuinely zero jobs" without a defensive pre-request cap.

**Pre-existing global config gaps surfaced again, not fixed** (same "flag, don't silently patch" discipline as the VP/Director substring bug across Waves 1–3): PepsiCo's real AI/ML postings use titles like "Senior Development Manager - AI Engineering" (killed by `exclude_terms`'s "manager") and "AI Solutions and Platforms Operations Engineer" (no `title_family` phrase matches AI-platform-shaped titles) — the same false-negative class already flagged for BlackRock/Moody's ("Vice President"/"Director" on genuine IC roles) and CitiusTech ("Lead Engineer - I/II" leveling). Also newly noted: Walmart's some JDs carry a boilerplate "languages/frameworks we also use" example paragraph that name-drops `.NET`/`C#` with zero relation to the actual role — a data-quality artifact in Walmart's own JD template, not a fetcher bug and not fixable via `require_tech_in_description` (same substring terms would still match). None of these were touched in this wave; they're recorded here as the growing backlog of `title_family`/`exclude_terms` precision work that would need a deliberate, reviewed global change across all 138 companies.

**Coordination held up under real load**: all 7 agents were launched in parallel (within the playbook's established 5-7 batch-size guidance) and all 7 hit the session rate limit simultaneously mid-investigation — same failure mode Wave 2/3 documented ("the session's rate-limit budget was simply more depleted by this point in a long, heavy day"). None had written any files yet at that point (`git status` showed a clean tree), so each was resumed from its own transcript once the limit reset rather than restarted cold — no lost work, no duplicated investigation. Two agents (Shell, Walmart) separately stalled later in their run (Shell: "no progress for 600s") after their fetcher was already built, tested, and working (confirmed by real state already on disk — a matched job ID in `seen_jobs_shell.json`) — resumed with an explicit instruction not to redo any investigation, just write the already-complete final report. Reinforces the Wave 2 lesson: **always check disk for real, working output before assuming a stalled/failed agent produced nothing** — it can be cheaper to resume than to relaunch.

All 163 tests pass (`test_registry_exactly_covers_fetchers_config_and_state`'s hardcoded count updated 131 → 138, same mechanical update every prior wave needed); `run_all.py --validate` confirms clean wiring for all 138 companies.

## Batch Onboarding Wave 6 (2026-09-03): 7 More GCCs — Disney, Expedia, GE Aerospace, GE HealthCare, Cisco, Nutanix, Schneider Electric

Follow-up to Wave 5's GCC research, which had identified more candidates than fit in one batch. The user asked to onboard "the other GCCs we had identified" — the remaining confirmed/plausible candidates from that research, again excluding Pune- and Chennai-only companies. **7 companies added, registry now covers 145**: Disney (Bangalore Development Center), Expedia Group, GE Aerospace (JFWTC), GE HealthCare, Cisco, Nutanix, Schneider Electric. (The weaker/unverified candidates from the same research — Qualcomm, AB InBev, Novartis, Delta Air Lines, Lufthansa, AstraZeneca, DAZN — were deferred to a follow-up wave rather than bundled in, per the same 5-7-agent batch-size discipline.)

**The "custom ATS guess is usually wrong" lesson from Wave 5 got dramatically stronger this wave: 6 of 7 pre-flagged guesses were wrong**, not 4 of 7. Disney → TalentBrew, Expedia → a brand-new vendor (WordPress + "Appcast Job Search" plugin), GE Aerospace → Workday hiding behind a Phenom People frontend skin, GE HealthCare → Phenom People frontend + Workday backend (corrected from a URL-shape "likely Oracle HCM CE" guess), Cisco → Phenom People, Schneider Electric → iCIMS/Jibe, Nutanix → a second brand-new vendor (Jobvite, corrected from "Greenhouse-adjacent"). Combined with Wave 5's 4/7, that's **11 wrong guesses against only 3 right ones (Nvidia/Shell/Pfizer, both waves) across 14 GCC onboardings** — added as a sharper standing note in the Key Bugs table: treat any research-sourced "ATS: X" claim as an unverified hypothesis, full stop, not a time-saving head start.

**A new specific pattern emerged from GE Aerospace and GE HealthCare (and retroactively explains Boeing/Barclays from earlier waves): a public careers page's own branding/CDN assets are not proof of which ATS actually holds the job data.** Both GE entities' public frontends are Phenom People, but each job's own `applyUrl` field revealed a completely different real backend (Workday) neither agent would have found by trusting the page's own branding alone. The reusable technique: when a page's pagination/search behaves oddly for its apparent ATS, or when in doubt at all, trace where the "Apply" button/field actually points before assuming the frontend's own usual integration pattern.

**Real matches confirmed across both tracks**: 25 total real matches this wave — Cisco alone contributed 20 (19 tagged `AI / ML / Python`, explicit generative-AI/LLM/Agentic-AI JD content, plus 1 genuinely hybrid `.NET / C#` + `AI / ML / Python` role naming both `.NET` and `generative ai`), Expedia contributed 2 (`AI / ML / Python`, explicit "generative ai"/"large language model"), and GE HealthCare contributed 3 (real primary-skill hits confirmed via `seen_jobs_gehealthcare.json`, consistent with it being a genuine medical-imaging-software GCC — track tags not re-verified in the final report pass, a 10-second follow-up if wanted). Disney, GE Aerospace, Nutanix, and Schneider Electric each returned a **genuine current zero** — same "confirmed-zero-is-a-real-fact" class as Boeing/Pfizer/PepsiCo from Wave 5: Disney's real engineering titles carry only broad-only skill mentions, GE Aerospace's entire India pool is 9 non-software jobs, Nutanix's and Schneider Electric's India engineering stacks (kernel/storage/infra; Java/Spring/Angular/embedded-IoT respectively) simply don't touch either track today.

**Pre-existing global config gaps kept surfacing, still not fixed**: Nutanix's "Engineering Manager – Agentic AI & SaaS" (genuinely naming LangChain/LangGraph/CrewAI/AutoGen/MCP) joins PepsiCo's "Senior Development Manager - AI Engineering" from Wave 5 as a second case of a real AI-engineering-flavoured posting killed purely by `exclude_terms`'s "manager" — now 2 companies in a row hitting this exact false-negative shape, on top of the pre-existing BlackRock/Moody's/CitiusTech instances. This backlog (documented, never silently patched) is getting large enough that a dedicated, deliberately-reviewed `title_family`/`exclude_terms` precision pass across all 145 companies is worth scheduling on its own, rather than accumulating more one-off flags.

**Coordination again hit rate limits, and again resumed cleanly rather than restarting**: all 7 agents launched in parallel; 5 hit the session rate limit mid-investigation and 1 stalled, leaving only Disney to complete on the first pass. All 6 were resumed from their own transcripts. New wrinkle this time: two agents (GE Aerospace, Expedia) had left real, already-useful temporary edits in `config.yaml`/`company_registry.py` when they were interrupted mid-revert — rather than have each resumed agent spend effort re-diffing and surgically removing just its own lines (risky when several agents are touching the same two files at once), the coordinator reset both shared files to clean baseline in one shot and told every resumed agent explicitly not to worry about reverting further, just re-add a temp entry if they still needed to test. This is a small but reusable process refinement over Wave 5's "each agent reverts its own edits" approach — **when several onboarding agents are all mid-flight on the same two shared files and some get interrupted before finishing their own revert, a single coordinator-side reset is more reliable than trusting each interrupted agent's own cleanup, especially across a resume boundary where the agent's completion signal ("done, cleaned up") clearly hadn't actually fired.**

All 163 tests pass (hardcoded count updated 138 → 145); `run_all.py --validate` confirms clean wiring for all 145 companies.

## Batch Onboarding Wave 7 (2026-09-03): The Final 7 GCCs — Qualcomm, AB InBev, Novartis, Delta Air Lines, Lufthansa, AstraZeneca, DAZN

Closes out the original GCC research from Wave 5: the 7 candidates deferred across Waves 5/6 for weaker initial signal — 3 with a plausible-but-unverified ATS guess (Qualcomm, AB InBev, Novartis) and 4 explicitly flagged low-confidence (Delta Air Lines via a thin "Talent500 intermediary" note, Lufthansa's "unclear ATS," AstraZeneca and DAZN as bare "plausible-unverified"). **All 7 added, registry now covers 152 companies.** With this wave, all 65 companies from the original 2026-08-30 target list plus the full GCC expansion the user asked for are now onboarded.

**The headline result: all 7 turned out feasible, including all 4 that were explicitly flagged as candidates for a clean "infeasible" report.** Delta's real presence wasn't the thin Talent500 lead at all — it's Delta's own dedicated "Delta Technology Hub" Avature microsite (`dth.avature.net`), found only by searching that branded name after the Talent500 lead and an Eightfold PCSX-disabled dead end both came up empty. Lufthansa's real presence is Lufthansa Systems India Private Ltd. (a third, distinct Group entity separate from Lufthansa Technik/Industry Solutions, both of which had zero open India reqs). AstraZeneca and DAZN each resolved cleanly to real, working platforms (TalentBrew and a newly-discovered Pinpoint integration respectively). **This reinforces the standing lesson from the original 2026-08-30 audit's wrongly-flagged-hard Google/Meta entries: initial secondary research sets triage priority, never feasibility** — even the thinnest lead deserves a real from-scratch investigation before being written off, and this wave produced zero Dream11/Toptal-style "not feasible" outcomes despite going in with the lowest-confidence batch of the whole GCC effort.

**Two more brand-new ATS vendors discovered, plus one new confirmed member of an existing platform family**: **Pinpoint** (DAZN — `<company>.pinpointhq.com/postings.json`, a clean unauthenticated JSON endpoint, no pagination, cache-once); a **Sense CRM domain fronting a Skillate career-page backend** (AB InBev — the prior "SenseHQ" guess was half right: the domain is genuinely Sense's candidate-engagement platform, but every bare route redirects to a login wall, while a separate public `/careers` Next.js micro-site on the same domain reports `"hosting_type":"SKILLATE"` in its own org config); and **milch & zucker's "Global Jobboard"** confirmed at a second tenant (Lufthansa, alongside Deutsche Bank's existing Beesite integration — same `LanguageCode`/`SearchParameters`/`SearchCriteria` request shape).

**Real matches confirmed across both tracks**: Delta contributed 5 (`AI / ML / Python`, explicit LLM-focused "AI Software Engineer" tiers), Qualcomm contributed 3-4 (`AI / ML / Python`, Agentic AI/Enterprise Gen AI/Multimodal ML roles, all Hyderabad), AstraZeneca contributed 1 (`AI / ML / Python`, a "Software Engineering Lead" explicitly naming LangChain/AutoGen/LlamaIndex/Pinecone/RAG) — 9-10 total. AB InBev, Novartis, Lufthansa, and DAZN each returned a **genuine current zero**, same "confirmed-zero-is-a-real-fact" class as every prior wave's zeros: AB InBev's board is analytics/ops/finance-heavy with one GenAI-adjacent posting that names no hard skill term; Novartis's and Lufthansa's real IT titles ("Associate Director - AI Infrastructure," "Platform Engineer," "Data Engineer") don't match any `title_family` phrase; DAZN's current Hyderabad stack (Node.js/TypeScript/AWS/Swift) has no `.NET/C#` or hard-AI signal today, though its "Conversational Developer" postings genuinely discuss Generative AI/Agentic AI/prompt engineering without matching `title_family` either.

**The `title_family`/`exclude_terms` precision backlog kept growing, still deliberately not touched**: this wave adds DAZN's "Conversational Developer" and Novartis's "Associate Director - AI Infrastructure" to the same false-negative family as PepsiCo/Nutanix's "manager"-excluded AI roles and BlackRock/Moody's "Vice President"/"Director" IC roles from earlier waves — now at least 6 companies across 3 waves hitting variations of the same two gaps (`exclude_terms` catching genuine IC titles, `title_family` missing real but unconventionally-shaped engineering titles). The backlog is large and consistent enough now that a dedicated, deliberately-reviewed precision pass across all 152 companies' real near-miss data is the clear next step, rather than continuing to accumulate isolated flags one company at a time.

**Coordination**: all 7 agents launched in parallel again hit the session rate limit simultaneously mid-investigation (the same pattern as every prior wave in this session) and were resumed cleanly using the Wave 6 refinement (a single coordinator-side reset of `config.yaml`/`company_registry.py` to clean baseline before resuming, rather than trusting each interrupted agent's own revert). No work was lost; every agent picked back up from its own transcript's last real progress rather than re-investigating from scratch.

All 163 tests pass (hardcoded count updated 145 → 152); `run_all.py --validate` confirms clean wiring for all 152 companies. Deployed and verified against a real triggered production run (see the commit history around 2026-09-03 for the exact verification): 152/152 companies succeeded end-to-end with real alerts sent for the newly-added companies, matching every onboarding agent's local findings exactly.

While reviewing Wave 5's original 22-candidate GCC research against what actually got onboarded across Waves 5–7, one confirmed candidate — **Salesforce** (dual-hub Bengaluru/Hyderabad) — turned out to have been silently dropped when the 22 candidates were split into three batches of 7 (a coordinator arithmetic error, not a deliberate exclusion). Caught only because the user asked "any companies left?" after Wave 7 closed out. **Added, registry now covers 153 companies.**

ATS confirmed as Workday-backed-by-a-static-CDN-export — see the Key Bugs entry above for the `jobs_1.json`/`jobs_2.json` gotcha. 3 real matches: 2 `AI / ML / Python` (explicit LangChain/vector-database/Pinecone and "large language model" mentions on AI-focused engineering titles) and 1 `.NET / C#` (C# named as one of several acceptable backend languages alongside Java/Python/Scala/Go — the same accepted-alternative-language pattern already treated as a legitimate match for Citi/ICE, not a false positive).

**Lesson**: when splitting a research list into fixed-size batches by hand, recount the total against the sum of the batches before considering the list closed — a silent off-by-one drop is easy to miss until someone asks "is that everything?"

## Near-Miss Audit Tool (2026-09-04)

After the GCC expansion completed (153 companies), the user relayed external feedback (via ChatGPT) arguing that fixing the matcher's precision/recall should come before any further company expansion — the same `title_family`/`exclude_terms` gap this playbook had already been flagging piecemeal since Wave 5 (BlackRock/Moody's, PepsiCo, Nutanix, DAZN, Novartis, CitiusTech). That feedback's specific title-list prescription turned out partly stale (checked live: `backend engineer`, `.NET developer`, `application developer`, `software developer` were already in `title_family`, and "Lead" was never blanket-excluded — those claims were inferred from the playbook's prose rather than the live config), but its meta-point was right: nobody should hand-pick title terms to add without first measuring what's actually being rejected and why. The corrected, agreed plan: **build a near-miss audit tool first, change nothing in the matcher yet.**

**What was built:**
1. **A small, additive logging change** (not a matching-logic change — no pass/fail decision is different) to `matcher.py` and `run_company.py`: every near-miss log line (`[exclude]`, `[title family]`, `[broad-only]`, `[react-only]`, `[skill]`, `[desc-tech]`) now ends with `[co=<company> id=<job id> loc=<location>]`. This exists specifically because `run_all.py` runs general (non-Playwright) companies concurrently in a shared thread pool — their near-miss print blocks interleave in the raw captured log, so "attribute by nearest preceding `[Company] Fetched:` line" (the obvious naive approach) silently misattributes rows. Embedding the company directly in each line sidesteps that instead of trying to reconstruct order after the fact.
2. **`src/near_miss_audit.py`** — pulls recent `watcher.yml` run logs via `gh run view --log`, parses every near-miss line, deduplicates by `(company, job_id)` (or `(company, title, stage)` when no id is available), and writes: `near_misses_deduped.csv`, `aggregate_by_stage.csv`, `aggregate_by_company.csv`, `title_family_candidate_impact.csv` (tests a short list of candidate `title_family` additions — `lead engineer`, `platform engineer`, `data engineer`, `devops engineer`, etc. — against real rejected titles using matcher.py's own normalization, so the result matches actual matcher behavior exactly, not a guess), `exclude_worth_review.csv` (a heuristic shortlist of `exclude`-tagged rejections killed by a "soft" term — manager/director/VP/principal/head of — the same false-negative shape already confirmed real at BlackRock/Moody's/PepsiCo/Nutanix), and a `summary.md`. Fully read-only — it changes no matcher/config behavior, only reports what already happened.
3. **A critical honesty fix during testing**: the first version attributed old (pre-tagging) log lines to "whichever company's `Fetched:` line most recently appeared" — tested against real logs, this produced plausible-looking but frequently wrong company names (exactly the interleaving risk describe above). Fixed by making old-format lines carry an explicit `UNATTRIBUTABLE (pre-tagging log)` company value instead of a guess — a wrong-but-plausible company name is worse than an honest "don't know" here, since it invites trusting a specific attribution that's actually a coin flip. Title text and rejection stage are unaffected by this and remain fully reliable even for old logs.
4. **`.github/workflows/near_miss_audit.yml`** — a `workflow_dispatch`-only workflow (not on the 30-min schedule) that runs the script and uploads its output directory as a downloadable GitHub Actions artifact (90-day retention), so future audits can be re-run on demand and diffed against a saved copy to build real trend history.
5. **6 new unit tests** (`tests/test_near_miss_audit.py`) covering the parser's new/old-format handling, the interleaving-safe fallback, dedup counting, and the title_family-impact analysis using matcher.py's real normalization.

**First real run's findings** (3 most recent runs, all pre-dating the tagging deploy so 100% low-confidence on company attribution — title text and aggregate counts are unaffected by that): 76,665 near-miss lines → 13,193 deduplicated unique rejections. By stage: `title family` 9,213, `exclude` 3,076, `broad-only` 515, `skill` 325. 270 distinct rejected titles would flip to passing under at least one candidate `title_family` addition — dominated by **`data engineer`** and **`platform engineer`** (both far more common in the real rejected-title corpus than the `lead engineer`/`lead developer` terms ChatGPT's original message emphasized), plus real volume on `devops engineer`. 2,659 `exclude` rejections were killed by a soft term worth a second look. **This is why the "measure first" plan mattered**: the two highest-impact real gaps (`data engineer`, `platform engineer`) weren't the ones anyone had guessed from memory.

**Explicitly not done in this pass**: no `title_family`/`exclude_terms` change was made to `config.yaml`. The audit output is for human review; any config change based on it should be a separate, deliberate decision — same discipline this playbook has followed for every other flagged-not-fixed gap.

## Batch Onboarding Wave 8 (2026-09-04): 7 Job-Search-Sourced Companies — Dover, HealthEdge, Deltek, Iris Software, Definitive Healthcare, Deltatre, Applied Systems

First wave sourced from real, scored job-search research rather than a GCC/company-list survey: the user provided a ranked "Tier 1" list of 10 specific *currently-open* roles (company, title, seniority band, required stack) scored on core-stack match, experience level, location, domain relevance, evidence strength, and interview bar. This wave onboarded the first 7 (highest-priority; the remaining 3 — Optym, Energy Exemplar, Payoneer — plus 11 Tier 2 companies are queued for a follow-up wave). **7 companies added, registry now covers 160.**

**Every agent was tasked with verifying its specific flagged role, not just onboarding the company generically** — each report had to state whether the exact posting the user was targeting was still open and would genuinely pass this repo's filters. All 7 came back with a positive confirmation on the flagged role, none infeasible. This is a meaningfully different success shape from the GCC waves (where most companies returned a genuine current zero) — these companies were selected specifically because they're .NET/AI-shaped shops matching the target candidate's actual stack, not broad multinationals where most roles are unrelated. **68 real matches surfaced in one wave** (vs. 21-25 per prior GCC wave of similar size): Iris Software alone contributed 45 (28 `.NET/C#`, 17 `AI/ML/Python`), HealthEdge 12, Applied Systems 4, Dover 3, Definitive Healthcare/Deltatre/Deltek 1-2 each.

**"Custom"/branded-domain guesses were wrong again, joined by two new failure shapes**: Dover's obvious `www.dovercareers.com` was Cloudflare-gated with no passable path at all (unlike NatWest, which does pass the same anti-detection technique) — the real ATS (`careers.dovercorporation.com`, SAP SuccessFactors J2W) was found only via a third-party aggregator link. Applied Systems' bare classic-iCIMS tenant blocked this repo's own shared scraper User-Agent via AWS WAF — not a TLS-fingerprint issue, confirmed via both `curl` and `requests`, worked around with a different UA. Both are new variants of the standing "the branded domain isn't proof of anything" lesson, not repeats of it.

**Two real, would-have-been-silent bugs were caught and fixed mid-onboarding, not just documented**: Iris Software's cache-once fetcher, built against an *unsorted* ~340-job pool, put the exact flagged target role at position 339 of 340 — under the default `max_listings: 200` it would have been silently dropped before ever reaching the matcher. Fixed by requesting a sort order server-side and raising `max_listings` above the known total as defense-in-depth. Deltek's BrassRing tenant exposes two title fields for the same posting — the friendlier one (`formtext4`, "AI Solutions Engineer") was tested, found to trip `exclude_terms`'s "solutions engineer" phrase, and silently cost a real LangChain/CrewAI/LLM match; the uglier abbreviated field (`jobtitle`, "Pncpl Software Engineer") was kept instead specifically because it doesn't cost the match. Both are concrete instances of "verify against real near-miss output before trusting the more convenient-looking option" — worth remembering as a general onboarding discipline, not just a fact about these two companies.

**Coordination**: all 7 agents launched in parallel hit the session rate limit simultaneously mid-investigation (now a completely routine pattern across every wave this session) and were resumed cleanly via the Wave 6/7 refinement (coordinator-side reset of `config.yaml`/`company_registry.py` before resuming, rather than trusting each interrupted agent's own cleanup).

All 171 tests pass (hardcoded count updated 153 → 160); `run_all.py --validate` confirms clean wiring for all 160 companies.

## Batch Onboarding Wave 9 (2026-09-04): 6 More Job-Search-Sourced Companies — Optym, Energy Exemplar, Payoneer, SITA, insightsoftware, YASH Technologies, Alegeus

Second wave from the same scored job-search research as Wave 8: the remaining 3 Tier 1 companies (Optym, Energy Exemplar, Payoneer) plus the first 4 "High" priority Tier 2 companies (SITA, insightsoftware, YASH Technologies, Alegeus). **6 of 7 added, registry now covers 166.** Optym came back genuinely infeasible (see below) — a legitimate outcome, not a shortfall.

**Every agent again verified its specific flagged role, and every onboarded company confirmed it** — same 100% hit rate as Wave 8. **30 more real matches** surfaced: Alegeus and YASH Technologies each contributed 8 (Alegeus: 6 `.NET/C#` + 2 hybrid; YASH: 6 `.NET/C#` + 2 `AI/ML/Python`), Energy Exemplar and Payoneer 4 each, SITA and insightsoftware 3 each — combined with Wave 8's 68, **98 real matches now surfaced across 13 job-search-sourced companies**, a completely different yield profile from the GCC waves.

**Optym is the first genuinely infeasible company since Toptal/Turing/Trilogy/Dream11** — and the investigation is worth noting for its thoroughness: the agent found and confirmed a real, working, never-before-seen ATS (BambooHR, `optym.bamboohr.com`), but that tenant's public careers list returns `totalCount: 0`. Rather than accept that at face value, the agent probed job-detail IDs 1–300 directly (all redirect to empty), cross-checked ~35 unrelated companies' BambooHR tenants (nearly all also empty, including BambooHR's own careers page, now on Greenhouse), and independently confirmed via LinkedIn's own `expired_jd_redirect` marker that the two specific postings the user had found are closed. Optym's public job distribution has moved entirely to LinkedIn, which this repo has no scraping precedent for. Correctly declared infeasible rather than shipping unverifiable parsing logic against a schema with zero live examples — a textbook case of the "don't force a broken fetcher" escape hatch working as intended.

**Two more real, would-have-been-silent bugs caught and fixed**: Energy Exemplar's Greenhouse board represents one requisition open in multiple cities as a single semicolon-separated location string (`"Bengaluru, Karnataka, India; Pune, Maharashtra, India"`) — the standard plain-substring `exclude_locations` check would have killed a valid Bengaluru posting purely because Pune also appeared in the same string; fixed with a per-segment location picker that prefers a non-excluded city rather than either blindly keeping or blindly dropping the whole string. insightsoftware's Workday tenant omits `locationsText` entirely (not blank — the key is simply absent) for ~5% of postings, including a genuine single-location India match; the standard `"india" not in loc` client-side filter would have silently dropped it, fixed with a one-off detail-page fallback lookup.

**Three more instances of "the branded domain/name isn't proof of anything," now spanning three distinct mechanisms**: insightsoftware's real Workday tenant name (`magnitudesoftware`) is a leftover from a 2021 acquisition that never got renamed — a new mechanism (M&A history) distinct from Macquarie's same-named-different-org confusion or GE Aerospace's frontend-skin-over-different-backend shape. Payoneer's bot-gated primary domain led, via `robots.txt`'s own `Sitemap:` line, to a third-party "TeamMe" career-site product that itself just proxies a standard Greenhouse board — the reusable technique (check `robots.txt` when the primary domain is gated) and tell (`?gh_jid=` in any apply link) are both new. Alegeus confirmed the *inverse* case worth watching for at any newly-acquired company: its own tenant is verified **not** absorbed into the acquirer's (Optum's) pipeline, still running independently 2 months after the acquisition closed — don't assume consolidation without checking.

**Coordination**: all 7 agents again hit the session rate limit simultaneously mid-investigation and were resumed cleanly via the now-standard coordinator-side shared-file reset. No work was lost, including Optym's — its agent had done all its investigation work before hitting the limit and simply needed to finish the writeup on resume.

All 171 tests pass (hardcoded count updated 160 → 166); `run_all.py --validate` confirms clean wiring for all 166 companies.

## Batch Onboarding Wave 10 (2026-09-05): Eurofins, SimCorp — Closing Out the Job-Search-Sourced List

Final two companies from the same scored job-search research as Waves 8–9: Eurofins (Tier 2 "High," healthcare/product environment) and SimCorp (Tier 2 "Medium," explicitly flagged by the user as likely to have "a tougher technical bar and fewer suitable openings"). **Both added, registry now covers 168** — this closes out the entire job-search-sourced list (Tier 1 fully done, first 6 of Tier 2 done; the remaining Tier 2 entries — Saxo Bank, The Hartford, Resideo, Luxoft, Omnissa — were deliberately not started this session per the user's direction).

**SimCorp's real result inverted the user's own "fewer openings" expectation**: 33 real matches, not the thin trickle the Tier 2 framing anticipated — a useful reminder that the scoring in the original research was a *prioritization* heuristic (why the user put this company second-tier), not a prediction of how many real matches would surface once actually measured. Eurofins likewise dramatically overperformed a routine "healthcare product company" expectation: 49 real matches, all `.NET/C#`, from a dedicated ~3000-person Bengaluru delivery center (EITSI) that most casual research would never surface behind a global lab-testing company's generic branding.

**Two more real, would-have-been-silent bugs caught and fixed**, both distinct new shapes: Eurofins' SmartRecruiters `region` field carries a genuine Indian state code (`"TN"`) for Coimbatore/Tiruppur postings whose city text itself never says "Tamil Nadu"/"Chennai" — would have leaked straight past `exclude_locations`; fixed by appending the state name from the region code before handing location off to the shared matcher. SimCorp's Workday tenant collapses ~15 of ~71 real India postings into an ambiguous "N Locations" label (the standard Workday multi-site-req shape already seen at Maersk) — but unlike Maersk, SimCorp's search has no location facet to pre-filter with at all, so blindly keeping or dropping every ambiguous posting would each be wrong in different directions (most real "N Locations" combinations here are non-India, e.g. Warsaw+Milano); resolved via a one-off detail-page country lookup per ambiguous posting during the cache fill, the only reliable signal available.

**A process note, not a technical one**: this wave was interrupted by an explicit user "stop" mid-launch (after only Eurofins and SimCorp had been dispatched, before the other 5 planned companies), then resumed with "continue" once the two in-flight agents' rate limit reset. The remaining 5 companies from the user's list were correctly *not* relaunched without explicit confirmation — matching the general principle that a scope-limiting instruction mid-task caps the task at what was already committed, not what was merely planned.

All 171 tests pass (hardcoded count updated 166 → 168); `run_all.py --validate` confirms clean wiring for all 168 companies. This closes out 15 total companies from the user's job-search research across Waves 8–10 (14 successfully onboarded, 1 — Optym — genuinely infeasible), surfacing 180 real matches combined.

## Batch Onboarding Wave 11 (2026-09-05): The Final 5 — Saxo Bank, The Hartford, Resideo, Luxoft, Omnissa

Closes out the entire job-search-sourced company list from Waves 8–11: the 5 Tier 2 entries deliberately deferred at the end of Wave 10 (per the user's explicit "stop"). **All 5 feasible, all 5 added — registry now covers 173.** Every one of the original 20 job-search-sourced companies has now been resolved (19 onboarded, 1 — Optym — genuinely infeasible).

Run as 5 fully parallel agents, each assigned one company and explicitly scoped to touch only its own new files (`src/<slug>_fetcher.py`, `seen_jobs_<slug>.json`) — none were allowed to edit `company_registry.py`/`config.yaml`/the test file directly, avoiding the parallel-merge-conflict problem prior waves hit. Each agent instead reported its exact registry fields and `config.yaml` block back for the coordinator to merge sequentially by hand once all 5 landed; zero conflicts resulted.

**29 real matches surfaced**: Luxoft 12 (5 `.NET/C#` + 7 `AI/ML/Python`, the widest spread of the five), The Hartford 9 (all but one hybrid-tagged, its Hyderabad GCC is genuinely AI-forward — a real current zero for pure `.NET/C#`-only), Saxo Bank 2 and Omnissa 4 (both `.NET/C#`-only, real current zeros for `AI/ML/Python` — Saxo's one AI role is Copenhagen HQ, not Gurugram), Resideo 2 (`.NET/C#`-only, off a tiny 7-job India board).

**Two new platform-discovery mechanisms, both distinct from anything in the existing "branded domain isn't proof of anything" list**: Resideo's real integration point is a first-party **Webflow Cloud serverless function** on the marketing site's own origin (`www.careers.resideo.com/app/api/jobs`) — found via a bundled `careercategorymap` JS file — fronting a genuine Oracle Fusion HCM tenant whose own REST search returns `TotalJobsCount: 0` under the URL's site slug (the real `siteNumber` was never recovered, so the Oracle tenant is a dead end and the Webflow proxy is the only working path). Luxoft is a **platform-not-tenant** variant of the pattern: DXC (its 2019 acquirer) absorbed Luxoft's *frontend platform and branding* (`career.luxoft.com` literally serves DXC's own in-house Vue.js career-site code, schema.org `hiringOrganization` reads "DXC Luxoft") but Luxoft kept its own fully independent, non-overlapping job pool (1211 global / 437 India) — distinct from this repo's existing `dxc_fetcher.py`, which talks to DXC's own separate Workday tenant. Saxo Bank added a third variant of "decoy front-end": its branded page embeds a widget CSS-themed "workday" that isn't Workday at all (a category-only aggregator), while the real Workday CXS tenant lives behind a separate "See job openings" link the decoy widget doesn't mention.

**One new WAF false-positive class, found empirically rather than assumed**: Luxoft's Azure Application Gateway WAF blocks (403) any keyword search whose first word is a lowercase interpreter name — `"python developer"`, one of this repo's own shared default keywords, is blocked outright, while `"Python developer"` (capital P) returns the identical result set. Found only because the agent ran the repo's actual default keyword list rather than synthetic test terms; fixed by upper-casing just the keyword's first character before the request.

**The `title_family`/`exclude_terms` precision backlog kept growing — again flagged, not fixed, per standing discipline**: five more real, currently-open postings were found and left unpatched pending the dedicated audit — The Hartford's two "Data Scientist" hybrid titles killed by the `"data scientist"` exclude term (one explicitly names LangChain/LangGraph/RAGAS/RAG); Resideo's "Backend -.net/C# and Azure Engineer" (non-adjacent phrase breaks the `title_family` match); Saxo Bank's "Site Reliability Engineer" and "DevOps Engineer" (zero practical alerting impact even if fixed — neither has a real `primary_skills` hit); Luxoft's two "Principal Software Engineer/Backend Engineer (Python)" postings killed by `exclude_terms`'s `"principal"` — a new trigger word in the same false-negative family as "manager"/"VP"/"director"/"Associate Director" from BlackRock/Moody's/PepsiCo/Nutanix/Novartis/DAZN; and Omnissa's "Member Technical Staff 3" (a brand-new title *shape*, not a leveling variant — explicitly requires RAG/vector-DB/LLM-agent experience) plus two "Performance Engineer" postings requiring .NET/C#. **This wave alone adds 6 more companies to the backlog (now 12+ across 5 waves), including the first new `exclude_terms` trigger word ("principal") since the original BlackRock/Moody's find** — reinforcing that the dedicated precision-pass audit flagged since Wave 7 is now overdue rather than optional.

All 171 tests pass (hardcoded count updated 168 → 173); `run_all.py --validate` confirms clean wiring for all 173 companies. This closes out all 20 companies from the user's original job-search research across Waves 8–11 (19 successfully onboarded, 1 — Optym — genuinely infeasible), surfacing 209 real matches combined.
