## The Deliberately-Reviewed `title_family`/`exclude_terms` Precision Pass (2026-09-05)

The dedicated audit flagged as "overdue" at the end of every wave since Wave 7 (BlackRock/Moody's, PepsiCo, Nutanix, DAZN, Novartis, CitiusTech, Eurofins, Resideo, Saxo Bank, Luxoft, Omnissa — 12+ companies across 6 waves, all hitting variations of the same two gaps). Run using the existing `near_miss_audit.py` tool (built for exactly this in an earlier session) against the last 20 completed `watcher.yml` runs — 523,366 raw near-miss log lines, deduplicated to 45,579 unique real (company, job) rejections spanning all 173 companies. This is the first time this backlog was addressed as one measured, evidence-based pass instead of accumulating more isolated per-company flags.

**`title_family` gained 20 new phrases**, in two groups. Eight were the audit tool's own pre-built candidate list (`lead engineer`, `lead developer`, `lead software engineer`, `platform engineer`, `data engineer`, `cloud engineer`, `devops engineer`, `conversational developer`) — already scored against real rejected titles by `title_family_candidate_impact.csv` (973 distinct titles would flip). Twelve more came directly from this wave's own onboarding findings and a fresh read of the real data: `C# developer`, `.NET engineer`, `C# engineer` (Eurofins/PepsiCo-style bare ".NET Engineer"/"C# Engineer" titles with no "software"/"developer" qualifier — `.NET developer` existed but its symmetric pairs didn't), `fullstack developer`, `fullstack engineer` (the concatenated-word form `_normalize_text` can't derive from the existing hyphenated/spaced phrases), `site reliability engineer`, `performance engineer`, `member of technical staff`, `member technical staff` (Saxo Bank/Omnissa). All of these are safe by construction: they only widen the Layer-2 title-family gate, and every job still has to clear Layer 3's `primary_skills` description check (real `.NET/C#` or `LangChain`/`RAG`/etc. content) before it can ever become an alert — confirmed by replaying all 45,579 real historical rejections through the new config: 1,272 titles are newly recoverable from `title_family` alone (top volumes: Accenture 123, TCS 94, IBM 85, HCLTech 82 — spot-checked and legitimate: "Data Engineer", "DevOps Engineer", "Cloud Platform Engineer", "Site Reliability Engineer", "Fullstack Developer").

**`exclude_terms` lost four terms — `principal`, `director`, `vice president`, `VP` — and gained none back.** This was the harder, higher-stakes call, made by actually reading real samples from `exclude_worth_review.csv` (7,301 rows flagged "worth review") rather than guessing. Real evidence across dozens of tagged rejections confirmed these four terms were killing genuine hands-on IC roles industry-wide, not just at the two originally-flagged companies: BlackRock alone had ~15 ("Application Engineer, Vice President", "Full Stack Engineer, Vice President", "Data Engineer, Vice President", "Director, Application Engineer" — all real Aladdin engineering ICs banded VP/Director, banking-convention titles, not management), plus Citi ("Senior AI/ML Engineer - Vice President", "Senior LLM and Agentic AI Engineer - Assistant Vice President"), Goldman Sachs ("GBM - Systematic Credit - Quantitative Engineering - VP"), Deutsche Bank ("Lead Engineer, VP", "Data Science and AI Engineering Lead, VP"), and this wave's own Luxoft ("Principal Software Engineer with Python") and Novartis ("Associate Director - AI Infrastructure") finds. This is safe for the same two-gate reason as the `title_family` additions — a title must *also* match `title_family` (a bare "VP, Business Finance" or "Director of Legal" never will) and the description must still name a real `primary_skills` term. **`data scientist` was also removed** — The Hartford's hybrid "Senior Software Engineer/Data Scientist" titles are genuine `.NET/C#`+`AI/ML/Python` engineering roles; a *pure* "Data Scientist" title still can't pass `title_family` regardless, so nothing was reopened there.

**`manager`/`engineering manager` were deliberately KEPT — the one real trade-off made, not silently dropped.** Two specific known-good counter-examples exist (PepsiCo's "Senior Development Manager - AI Engineering", Nutanix's "Engineering Manager – Agentic AI & SaaS", both independently confirmed via live JD content in Waves 5/6 to be genuine LangChain/CrewAI-naming IC roles). But sampling the same live audit data found a far larger and more consistent pattern of "Manager" as a *genuine* people-management signal that would otherwise slip straight past `title_family`'s loose substring matching — purely because "Engineering" contains "engineer" as a literal prefix (`"software engineering manager"` contains the substring `"software engineer"`): Atlassian's "Senior Engineering Manager, Search Infrastructure", "Senior Data Engineering Manager"; eBay's "Data Engineering Manager", "Senior Engineering Manager"; CRISIL's "Senior Data Platform Engineering Manager"; FactSet's "Engineering Manager - Modern Data Pipeline"; Chubb's "Engineering Manager -Full Stack(NodeJS)" — real managers, every one. Removing `manager` would have recovered 2 known roles at the cost of reopening this much larger class (5,086 raw near-miss lines carried a "manager"-family term — the single largest soft-term bucket by volume). PepsiCo's and Nutanix's 2 specific titles remain a documented, deliberate residual gap: known, not silently dropped, just not safely fixable with a global term change. `head of` was left untouched too — sampling found zero engineering-titled false negatives behind it (its real hits were all Marketing/Legal/GTM/Design leadership titles that `title_family` already rejects on their own).

**One structural gap explicitly NOT fixed, flagged instead**: Resideo's "Backend -.net/C# and Azure Engineer" (Wave 11) still fails `title_family` because "Backend" and "Engineer" aren't adjacent in the title — the shared substring-matching approach has no adjacency tolerance, and adding one risks new false positives elsewhere (e.g. "Backend Support ... Engineer" collisions). Left as a known limitation rather than forcing a riskier architecture change for one title.

`near_miss_audit.py` itself was updated to stay accurate: `_HARD_EXCLUDE_TERMS`/`_SOFT_EXCLUDE_TERMS` now mirror the real config (no longer classify `principal`/`director`/`vice president`/`VP`/`data scientist`, which will never appear in a real `[exclude]` log line again), and `_CANDIDATE_TITLE_FAMILY_ADDITIONS` — its job done — was emptied out with a comment explaining it's meant to be repopulated by the *next* precision pass, not a permanent record of this one. `tests/test_near_miss_audit.py`'s normalization test was updated to monkeypatch its own candidate list rather than depend on the module's real (now-empty) one, the same decoupling `test_matcher.py` already used for its own `TITLE_FAMILY`/`EXCLUDE` fixtures.

All 171 tests pass; `run_all.py --validate` confirms clean wiring for all 173 companies. No company-specific `config.yaml` blocks or fetchers were touched — this pass only changed the shared `matching` section and `near_miss_audit.py`.

## Batch Onboarding Wave 12 (2026-09-06): Mid-Cap IT Services — Zensar, Birlasoft, Coforge, Happiest Minds, Cyient

5 companies onboarded. Sonata Software was investigated in the same batch but deferred (agent hit session rate limit; no fetcher created yet — will be onboarded in the next wave).

**ATS breakdown:**
- **Zensar Technologies** — Oracle HCM Cloud CXS REST API (`fa-etvl-saasfaprod1.fa.ocs.oraclecloud.com`, site `CX_1`). Keywords genuinely narrow server-side (confirmed by comparing response counts for nonsense vs real tokens) — NOT registered in `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`. An important gotcha unique to this tenant: an empty keyword returns 0 jobs (not the full pool like Hexaware/Chubb-style CXS tenants) — so the fetcher must always pass a real keyword. India scoped server-side via `selectedLocationsFacet`. 13 matches across the default keyword set.
- **Birlasoft** — SAP SuccessFactors J2W (`jobs.birlasoft.com`, company ID `birlasoftl`). `locationsearch=India` filter is reliable (confirmed zero non-India leakage across the full ~591-job pool). Keywords ignored (full pool cached once — same "cache-once" reasoning as UBS/Deutsche/Persistent). Coimbatore added to `exclude_locations`: Birlasoft has a real office there ("INDIA-COIMBATORE-BIRLASOFT OFFICE, IN") but its location text never contains "Tamil Nadu" or "Chennai" — the same silent-city-leak class already handled for Eurofins/HealthEdge/State Street/Razorpay. 2 matches.
- **Coforge** (formerly NIIT Technologies) — Zwayam ATS (`public.zwayam.com`, companyId base64 `MTUxNzM=` = 15173). Third Zwayam `public.zwayam.com` tenant after CRISIL (15438) and Persistent's own subdomain. 70 of 113 total postings are India-located (country field `"INDIA"` in structured `jobLocationRecord`). Registered in `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`. 2 matches.
- **Happiest Minds** — Zwayam ATS (`public.zwayam.com`, companyId base64 `MTU5NzQ=` = 15974). 323 of 360 company-wide jobs are India-located. `hasMoreData` terminates correctly (no wraparound observed). Titles are ALL-CAPS by Zwayam tenant convention — harmless since matcher normalises case. Registered in `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`. 5 matches.
- **Cyient** — Zwayam ATS (`public.zwayam.com`, companyId base64 `MTU0ODY=` = 15486). Investigation path: `content-security-policy` named `*.zwayam.com`; Naukri webhook reference confirmed Zwayam as the vendor; bundle's `environment` object gave API host and companyId. 96 India jobs. Empty-string keyword always passed to API (fetcher hardcodes `anyOfTheseWords: ""`); config uses `keywords: [""]` to document this explicitly (valid under the validation rule because `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS` exempts the non-empty-string check). Registered in `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`. 2 matches.

## Batch Onboarding Wave 13 (2026-09-06): 8 Companies Across Mid-Cap IT, Insurance GCC, and Dev-Tool SaaS

Dispatched 6 parallel batches (~29 candidate companies) covering mid-cap IT services, GlobalLogic/Globant-style engineering services, insurance/HR-tech GCCs, dev-tool SaaS, and Big 4 India delivery centers. Two batches (Chargebee/Innovaccer/Whatfix/Zoho/CleverTap/MoEngage, and Deloitte USI/PwC AC/EY GDS/KPMG Global Services) hit the session rate limit before completing and are deferred to a future wave. Two batches (KPIT/LTTS/GlobalLogic/Globant/EPAM/ThoughtWorks) were still running as of this entry. This entry covers the two batches that finished: insurance/HR-tech GCCs (Batch 5) and dev-tool/consultancy SaaS (Batch 3).

**Batch 5 — insurance & HR-tech GCCs: 2 of 6 feasible.**
- **Aon** — iCIMS REST API (`jobs.aon.com/api/jobs`). Both `keywords` and `location=India` are genuine server-side filters (verified: nonsense keyword → 0; India facet → 42 of the global pool). Descriptions fully inline in the search response — `fetch_job_description` raises `NotImplementedError`. Pagination is `page=N` (1-indexed) + `limit`; `offset` does not work. 4 matches for "software engineer" (Bengaluru-based Full Stack/Engineering Lead roles).
- **Zurich Insurance** — SAP SuccessFactors J2W classic HTML (`careers.zurich.com/search/`), same family as Swiss Re/Nomura/Capgemini. Both `q=` (keyword) and `locationsearch=india` genuinely filter server-side. **Currently 0 India jobs** — Zurich's Hyderabad GCC only launched April 2026 and hasn't posted openings yet; the fetcher is onboarded pre-emptively so it self-activates the moment roles appear, without needing a future onboarding pass.
- **Darwinbox, Allianz Technology**: both Cloudflare-gated (403 to plain `requests`, would require Playwright) — deferred, not onboarded this wave.
- **Liberty Mutual, Travelers**: confirmed **zero India presence** (Liberty Mutual: `location=India` returns 0 via its Eightfold/Findly API; Travelers: Workday facets list only Canada/Ireland/UK/US, no India) — permanently out of scope, not a fetcher gap.

**Batch 3 — dev-tool & consultancy SaaS: 6 of 6 feasible.**
- **Sopra Steria** — SmartRecruiters (`SopraSteria1` tenant, same platform family as Nagarro/Eurofins/NECSWS/PhonePe). `country=in` hardcoded server-side (single-country facet, `location` param unused — same pattern as Zensar). Keywords genuinely narrow server-side (0 for nonsense token). ~113 India postings.
- **Publicis Sapient** — iCIMS Jibe REST via `careers.publicisgroupe.com/api/jobs` (Publicis Groupe's *parent* portal, not a Sapient-branded domain — same iCIMS Jibe family as Gallagher/Schneider Electric/S&P Global Careers). `location=India` genuinely filters server-side (added to `_SUPPORTS_LOCATION`) — but **keywords are deliberately NOT passed to the API**: the endpoint serves every Publicis Groupe brand, and keyword-filtered results are overwhelmingly non-Sapient (mostly Epsilon). A brand filter on `apply_url` tenant / `tags2` isolates ~60 genuine Sapient India postings from the ~200-job total Publicis Groupe India pool. Descriptions fully inline.
- **Freshworks** — SmartRecruiters (same tenant family as Sopra Steria). `country=in` hardcoded, keywords genuine server-side filter. ~48 India postings (Chennai, Bengaluru).
- **Postman** — Greenhouse (`boards-api.greenhouse.io/v1/boards/postman/jobs?content=true`). No server-side keyword or location filter at all — full ~63-job global pool cached once, India filtered client-side via `location.name`. Descriptions fully inline via `?content=true`. Only 4 India postings (Bengaluru/Hyderabad), all genuine engineering roles.
- **BrowserStack** — Workday CXS (`browserstack.wd3.myworkdayjobs.com`). `searchText` genuinely narrows server-side; no usable location facet exists (job family/worker type/time type only) so India is detected client-side via a Mumbai city allowlist (same pattern as SimCorp/Genpact). Server hard-caps `limit` at 20 (25+ returns HTTP 400). Board is currently mostly Sales/Ops (0 engineering matches today) — a genuine current fact, fetcher will pick up India engineering roles once posted.
- **Xoriant** — Oracle Taleo Recruiting CE (`xoriant.taleo.net`, legacy FTL portal). Tiny, unusual board: 15 total requisitions exist in the Taleo database but only 1 is configured "posted externally" and visible via the public REST API (a networking QA role — 0 .NET/C#/AI/Python matches today). Xoriant is India-only, so every visible posting is treated as India-based with no location filtering needed. The Taleo FTL detail page is JS-rendered with no server data, so `fetch_job_description` returns a skills/tags proxy string pulled from the search response instead of a real per-job fetch.

## Batch Onboarding Wave 13 conclusion (2026-09-06): India Product SaaS + Big 4 GCCs

Closes out Wave 13. Both remaining batches completed on their relaunch attempt.

**Batch 4 — India product SaaS: 5 of 6 feasible.**
- **Innovaccer** — Workable public widget API. **Account slug is `innovaccer-analytics`, not `innovaccer`** (the obvious slug 404s) — found by following an `apply.workable.com/j/{shortcode}` link from the careers page rather than guessing. No server-side keyword/location filter — full 75-job pool (31 India) cached once. Descriptions via Workable's Markdown export endpoint (a real per-job fetch, not inline).
- **Whatfix** — Trakstar Hire (RecruiterBox), tenant `whatfix101`, discovered by reading the site's own deferred `WhatfixAPI.js` rather than guessing a tenant slug. Uses the full-board RSS feed (no query params — same board every call). 31 total/28 India jobs. Descriptions inline in the feed. Gotcha: `locationCity` sometimes already contains a full "City, State, India" string — the location builder checks for an existing "india" substring before appending to avoid "...India, India".
- **Zoho Corporation** — Zoho's own self-hosted "Career Site" product, not a third-party ATS. List data is HTML-entity-escaped JSON embedded in a hidden `<input id="jobs">`; full descriptions require a per-job detail page using a *different* JS-string escaping style (hex `\x22` escapes), handled with a dedicated decoder. **Only 2 total postings company-wide right now** (neither matching this repo's tracked keywords) — confirmed genuine via the page's own "Current Openings" label, same low-volume precedent as PolicyBazaar/ING/eClerx, not a fetcher bug. No posting-date field exists anywhere on the site.
- **CleverTap** — The obvious lead (`clevertap.darwinbox.in`) is dead: Cloudflare bot management now blocks it platform-wide, confirmed by testing even this repo's own previously-documented-open reference Darwinbox tenant (`zepto.darwinbox.in`), which now returns the same challenge. The real, current board is a Kula.ai-hosted page (`careers.kula.ai/clevertap`) with no bot protection — found instead of reaching for Playwright. Data is a Next.js React Server Components "flight" payload, parsed by decoding `self.__next_f.push()` chunks and resolving `"$N"` backreferences against `N:T{hexlen},` text chunks — the most fragile fetcher onboarded this wave (undocumented internal framework serialization, not a real API), so every parse step degrades to empty output rather than crashing if Kula changes their page structure; only network failures raise `RateLimitError`. 17 total/11 India jobs, descriptions inline.
- **MoEngage** — Trakstar Hire, tenant `moengage`, same RSS pattern as Whatfix. 24 total/16 India jobs, descriptions inline. Same city-already-contains-India duplication guard as Whatfix.
- **Chargebee** — **infeasible, not onboarded.** Every `/careers/*` page on chargebee.com routes applicants exclusively to LinkedIn Jobs — no structured public job board exists. A legacy Trakstar Hire tenant does resolve but holds exactly one stale 2014 posting (confirmed abandoned). Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby/SmartRecruiters slug probes all 404 or return empty. No feasible public ATS API exists for this company today.

**Batch 6 — Big 4 India GCC/delivery centers: 4 of 4 feasible.**
- **Deloitte USI** — `apply.deloitte.com`/`jobsearch.deloitte.com` looked promising from secondary research but turned out to be a US-only Oracle Taleo section and a dead domain respectively — neither is India-relevant. The real board is `southasiacareers.deloitte.com` (SAP SuccessFactors J2W classic, same platform family as Nomura/Capgemini), linked from `www2.deloitte.com/in/en/careers.html`, with a dedicated India-only board node (`/go/Deloitte-India/718244/`) alongside sibling Mauritius/Sri-Lanka-and-Maldives nodes for the same South Asia member firm. ~1,678 total postings, confirmed India via `locationsText` ending ", IN". **Keyword search on this tenant is a noisy full-text OR-match, not real filtering** — adding a third word to the query *increases* match count rather than narrowing it (plausibly matching the full JD body's HR boilerplate, not just the title) — so keywords are ignored and the full pool is cached once.
- **EY GDS** — Same SuccessFactors J2W classic platform as Deloitte USI (identical `BS3ColumnizedSearch` CSS signature), but EY has no separate India-only board node — `careers.ey.com/ey/search/` is a single global board (7,976 jobs) scoped to India via `locationsearch=India` (2,342 India postings, confirmed via genuine `IN` country codes and Indian postal codes in the results, not a keyword-match artifact). Has the same noisy OR-match keyword problem as Deloitte USI, "only worse" — ".NET developer" alone returns ~98% of the entire India pool — so keywords are ignored here too.
- **KPMG Global Services** — `kpmg.com/in/en/careers.html` links to two different systems: a generic "experienced professionals" TalentRecruit board (not pursued — not the GCC entity this task targets) and the real "KPMG Global Services Careers" site on Oracle HCM Cloud CE (`ejgk.fa.em2.oraclecloud.com`, site `CX_3`) — KPMG's ~24,000-person GCC/GDC organization. Unlike most Oracle HCM CE tenants in this repo, **this entire board is already India-only** (699 total, exactly matching the India location facet count) — no location filter needed. Keyword genuinely narrows server-side (not a noisy OR-match) and is kept. Load-bearing quirk: omitting `expand=requisitionList.workLocation,requisitionList.secondaryLocations` makes the API silently return an empty `requisitionList` for every sort order while still reporting the correct `TotalJobsCount` — Chubb's near-identical fetcher already sends this expand param for an unrelated reason, but here it's required just to get results at all.
- **PwC Acceleration Centers** — Workday CXS (`pwc.wd3.myworkdayjobs.com/Global_Experienced_Careers`), a single global tenant (~4,598 jobs worldwide). The standard `locationCountry` facet that most Workday tenants in this repo use is **silently ignored** by this specific tenant (confirmed live: applying it still returned a job located in Cairo, Egypt) — India is scoped instead via 33 explicit per-city location WIDs passed directly in `appliedFacets.locations` (same city-WID technique as Barclays/Maersk, just a larger set), confirmed exact by cross-checking the summed per-WID counts against the combined query's `total`. Unlike Deloitte USI/EY GDS in this same batch, `searchText` genuinely narrows server-side here — real per-keyword filtering, not a noisy OR-match.

- `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`: added `clevertap`, `deloitteusi`, `eygds`, `innovaccer`, `moengage`, `whatfix`, `zoho` (kept off: `kpmgglobal`, `pwcac` — both genuinely filter server-side)

## Wave 14 (2026-09-06): Darwinbox — a "requires Playwright" rejection was wrong, corrected

Batch 5 (Wave 13) had marked Darwinbox's own careers page "infeasible" solely because its API returned Cloudflare 403s to plain `requests` calls. **That reasoning was invalid** — this repo already has 10 companies (bnpparibas, honeywell, ibm, natwest, perfios, servicenow, sonatasoftware, techmahindra, virtusa, and now darwinbox) onboarded specifically by using headless Firefox via Playwright to get past exactly this class of gate; "would require Playwright" is not a valid reason to skip a company here. Corrected by relaunching with an explicit instruction to use the established Playwright browser-singleton pattern.

**Darwinbox** (the HR-tech vendor's own careers page — distinct from the many *other* companies in this repo that merely use Darwinbox as their ATS) — tenant `dbx` (`dbx.darwinbox.in`), the newer `candidatev2` SPA tier (same as Perfios, not Zomato/Sonata's older `candidate` SPA). Real API: `POST /ms/candidateapi/job/alljobs?companyId=main`. Cloudflare blocks a bare `requests.post()` by User-Agent alone; Playwright is used both because a realistic UA alone isn't reliable from CI (GitHub Actions runner IPs get harsher treatment) and to match this repo's established convention for this class of tenant. 35 total jobs, 18 India, 0 matches today (current openings are Payroll/CS/Sales/Legal/Finance roles plus two AI-adjacent titles that don't literally match any configured `title_family` phrase — verified against the real matcher logic, a genuine current fact not a bug). Keywords/location ignored server-side — full pool cached once.

One gotcha worth flagging for future Playwright-based fetchers: this tenant's `/ms/candidateapi/job/alljobs` endpoint is a JSON POST, which triggers a CORS preflight that a blank `about:blank` page (sonata's per-call `new_page()` idiom) can't satisfy — fixed by keeping the *same* page that navigated to `dbx.darwinbox.in` open and reusing it for all API calls (same-origin, no preflight).

**Registry/config changes:**
- `_PIPELINE_DATA`: added `darwinbox`
- `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`, `_USES_PLAYWRIGHT`: added `darwinbox`
- `config.yaml`: added `darwinbox_search`
- Test count: 202 → 203; README: 202 → 203 companies

All 171 tests pass; `run_all.py --validate` confirms clean wiring for all 203 companies.

## Wave 14 continued (2026-09-06): Allianz Technology — also wrongly marked infeasible, and Playwright wasn't even needed

Closes out the Batch 5 correction. The original "infeasible" verdict tested the SPA's client-side widget API (`/api/apply/v2/jobs`, which genuinely does require a session/CSRF token) and stopped there. The actual fix needed no Playwright at all: a **cold, single-shot `requests.get()`** against `careers.allianz.com/global/en/search-results?keywords=...` returns the full result set as SSR JSON embedded in `phApp.ddo.eagerLoadRefineSearch` — the same Phenom People pattern already used for Cisco/United Airlines in this repo. Worth remembering: a Phenom tenant's *client-side widget* API failing is not proof the *page itself* isn't server-rendered — check the page's own SSR payload before reaching for Playwright.

**Allianz Technology** — Phenom People, tenant scoped by business `unit == "Allianz Technology"` (the group-wide board mixes in Allianz Partners and Allianz Services postings too — filtering by unit is required, not optional). `country=`/`location=` params are silent no-ops on this tenant; only `keywords=` genuinely narrows, and even that is an exact contiguous-phrase match (several real default keywords legitimately return 0, e.g. `.NET developer`, `dot net`, `python developer` — a real fact about current postings, not a bug). Fetcher therefore ignores the per-call `keyword`/`location` args entirely, always queries the one confirmed-reliable `keywords=India`, and lets matcher.py's own filters do the real work — same idiom as this repo's Darwinbox/Morningstar/ING fetchers. ~27-28 India postings (of ~1634 group-wide), 3 title matches today (2 "Senior Software Engineer", 1 "Senior One Identity Angular Developer").

**Registry/config changes:**
- `_PIPELINE_DATA`: added `allianztech`
- `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`: added `allianztech`
- `config.yaml`: added `allianztech_search`
- Test count: 203 → 204; README: 203 → 204 companies

All 171 tests pass; `run_all.py --validate` confirms clean wiring for all 204 companies. Both Batch 5 companies wrongly marked infeasible for "requires Playwright"/session-auth reasons are now corrected — the lesson for future batches: neither Cloudflare/bot-gating nor a client-side widget API failing is a valid reason to mark a company infeasible in this repo, since Playwright (10 companies) and SSR-payload scraping (this fix) both already have working precedent here.

## Wave 15 (2026-09-06/07): FAANG completion, dev-tool/cloud unicorns, and major Indian private banks

User-prompted gap audit: confirmed Apple and Netflix missing from FAANG coverage, and zero coverage of India's major private-sector banks despite this repo covering dozens of global banks and IT-services GCCs. Dispatched two 8-company batches; both nearly finished before hitting a session rate limit (ICICI Bank was the one casualty, relaunched separately — see below).

**FAANG + cloud/dev-tool unicorns:**
- **Apple** — jobs.apple.com's own server-rendered search (React Router v7 hydration payload in plain HTML, no Playwright). `location=india-INDC` and `search=` both genuinely filter server-side; keyword matching is OR-across-tokens (special-char tokens like "C#"/".NET" get stripped by the tokenizer to their plain-word equivalent) — still genuine narrowing, not the same as ignoring. 176 India postings.
- **Netflix** — explore.jobs.netflix.net's own server-rendered search (Eightfold platform, but unlike HSBC's Eightfold tenant in this repo, the public search API being disabled doesn't matter here because the page itself embeds live results server-side). Both `query=` and `location=` genuinely filter server-side. No working pagination — the embed hard-caps at 10 positions with `start=`/`num=` silently ignored; immaterial since India (8 of 501 global) fits well under that cap.
- **Uber** — custom Next.js site, Playwright required (CSRF-protected search API + Cloudflare-challenged detail pages). **Shipped with a real bug found and fixed in this session, not just onboarded blind**: the first draft's location-combobox interaction timed out 100% of the time in live testing — traced to a cookie-consent "Accept All" overlay intercepting pointer events on the (structurally visible/enabled) input underneath it. Fixed by dismissing the banner once per fresh page load before any combobox interaction. Worth remembering for any future Uber-style fetcher: a locator reporting visible/enabled/a-valid-bounding-box is not proof nothing is covering it — an overlay can still fail Playwright's actionability check silently until you check for one directly.
- **Stripe, Databricks, MongoDB, Cloudflare, GitLab** — five separate Greenhouse boards, same "cache-once, ignore query params" family already established for Glean/Groww/Razorpay/AlphaSense in this repo (verified per-tenant: a nonsense `q=` param always returns the identical full board). Each has its own location-field quirk worth remembering: Stripe's Bengaluru spelling is wildly inconsistent ("Bengaluru"/"Bangalore"/"IN-Bengaluru"/etc.); MongoDB's is a bare city name with no "india" substring (Gurugram/Bengaluru); **Cloudflare's `location.name` is actively misleading** — it's a work-mode label ("Hybrid"/"Remote") for most postings, not a place name at all, and the real signal is a separate `metadata["Job Posting Location"]` field; GitLab and Databricks both already say "India" cleanly with no normalization needed.
- **Snowflake** — real backing ATS is Ashby, reached by following `applyUrl` off Snowflake's own Phenom-fronted careers page rather than trusting Phenom's own search (its `location=` param is a geocode-radius search where the bare country name "India" fails to geocode and silently returns 0 — a dead end independently discovered here and worth remembering for any other Phenom tenant in this repo). Ashby's public board API needs no auth and ignores all query params — full pool cached once, `descriptionHtml` inline.
- **Workday** (the company itself, hiring for itself) — same Workday CXS API this repo already uses as an ATS *vendor* for a dozen other companies (browserstack/aon/pwcac/etc.), just self-hosted for their own hiring. Unlike most Workday tenants here, this one exposes a genuinely reliable `Location_Country` India facet.
- **Canva** — branded careers site is Cloudflare-gated, but the underlying SmartRecruiters API host has no such wall — same "branded domain isn't the real backend" lesson as several companies before it. `country=in` hardcoded (single-country facet); `q=` genuinely narrows (note: the plausible-looking `keyword=` param name is silently a no-op — only `q=` works).
- **Rippling** — real ATS is Rippling's own product (dogfooded), reached via a public-key Algolia index embedded in the site's JS bundle. Deliberately does NOT use Algolia's own relevance search for India detection: querying `"India"` returns a genuine false positive (a real Indianapolis, IN posting matches on the substring) — the same false-positive class this repo's own `is_india_job()` would also fall for if the raw query were ever used as the location signal. Full board fetched once, filtered by structured `countryCode` instead.

**Major Indian private-sector banks — a whole category with zero prior coverage:**
- **HDFC Bank, Axis Bank** — both RippleHire-backed (same vendor/API shape as CitiusTech/LTIMindtree/Mphasis already in this repo: `POST /candidate/candidatejobsearch`). Keyword is **required** on this vendor (an empty search returns 0, not the full board) and genuinely narrows server-side, but loosely/OR-based across tokens — e.g. Axis's "machine learning engineer" surfaces ~2,900 results by matching "machine"/"learning"/"engineer" separately across its large branch/RM/teller pool. Over-inclusive, not under-inclusive — safe, since matcher.py's title/skill filters downstream do the real precision work. Both are single bank-wide tenants (not IT-only boards); HDFC's signal-to-noise is noticeably cleaner than Axis's, with real "Tech & Digital-*Engineer*" titles surfacing directly and unusually rich JDs (literally listing "C# / VB.NET / JAVA / Xamarin / ASP.NET / .Net Core / Python / Angular / React" skill sections).
- **Kotak Mahindra Bank** — Oracle HCM Cloud CE (same REST pattern as Chubb/Hexaware/Icertis/WTW/eClerx). Same "keyword required" quirk as the RippleHire banks: an empty keyword returns `TotalJobsCount: 0` on this tenant specifically (not proof the board is empty) — never hit in practice since `fetch_jobs()` is always called with a real keyword.
- **ICICI Bank** — investigated in a separate pass after the original batch hit a rate limit mid-company; see below for outcome.

**A structural lesson from this wave, worth remembering for every future batch**: don't trust a sub-agent's own "should work" framing on an untested interactive Playwright flow — verify it live. Uber's fetcher shipped with an honest, explicit disclaimer in its own docstring that the combobox flow had never been exercised in a real browser (the agent's sandbox had no Chromium available), and live-testing it here immediately reproduced a 100%-repro failure the agent could not have caught itself. The fix took minutes once verified interactively; the alternative was silently merging a fetcher that would have returned 0 jobs forever while looking correctly wired in every other respect (imports cleanly, matches the contract, passes `run_all.py --validate`).

**Registry/config changes:**
- `_PIPELINE_DATA`: added `apple`, `axisbank`, `canva`, `cloudflare`, `databricks`, `gitlab`, `hdfcbank`, `kotakbank`, `mongodb`, `netflix`, `rippling`, `snowflake`, `stripe`, `workday`
- `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`: added `cloudflare`, `databricks`, `gitlab`, `mongodb`, `rippling`, `snowflake`, `stripe`
- `_SUPPORTS_LOCATION`: added `apple`, `netflix` (both genuinely use the passed `location` argument, unlike most single-country-facet fetchers in this repo which hardcode India instead)
- `_INLINE_DESCRIPTIONS`: added `cloudflare`, `databricks`, `gitlab`, `mongodb`, `snowflake`, `stripe`
- `config.yaml`: added a `_search` block for each of the 14 companies above
- Test count: 204 → 218; README: 204 → 218 companies

All 171 tests pass; `run_all.py --validate` confirms clean wiring for all 218 companies.

## Wave 15 conclusion (2026-09-07): Uber's three-bug saga, and ICICI Bank's AES-encrypted API

Closes out Wave 15. Both companies below hit real, non-trivial obstacles that a "looks correctly wired" check alone would never have caught.

**Uber — shipped broken, fixed through live iteration, not a single round.** The first draft's docstring honestly disclosed that its interactive combobox flow had never been exercised in a real browser (no local Chromium available during onboarding). Live-testing it here immediately reproduced a 100%-repro failure, and fixing it took three successive rounds, each surfacing a genuinely different bug once the previous one was patched:
1. **A cookie-consent overlay blocked every interaction.** `[data-slot="dialog-overlay"]` intercepted pointer events on the location combobox even though the combobox itself reported visible/enabled with a valid bounding box — Playwright's actionability check just silently retries for 30s and times out, it doesn't surface "covered by X" unless you read the full retry log. Worse: because the site's location "search" never does a real page navigation, a dialog stuck open from one failed dismiss persisted across every subsequent city in the scrape loop, not just the first.
2. **Selecting a location and setting the radius never actually applied the filter.** There's a separate, exact-match "Search" button that must be clicked to submit the query — a *different*, unrelated header button confusingly also says "Search jobs" and does nothing to this form. Without the real click, the page kept silently showing its default "10 newest jobs company-wide" feed, which is shape-compatible with real results (same card count, same DOM structure) until you actually check the locations (Sydney/Mexico/Chicago/Krakow — obviously not India, but not obviously *wrong* from the code's own perspective).
3. **Even a successful-looking URL wasn't proof the filter took.** `page.url` gaining a `location=` param is not reliable evidence on this site — confirmed live, it can retain a *stale* param from a previous city's successful search even when the current city's search silently failed. The fix that actually worked: read the real visible card locations and require at least one to genuinely mention India or a known hub city before trusting that city's results at all (`_results_look_india_filtered()`); if not, skip that city with a loud warning instead of accepting wrong data. Confirmed live: a run where 5 of 7 hub-city searches failed this check still produced a 100%-correct final result set, because Bengaluru's own 1000-mile search radius alone already covers most of India — a skipped city costs some recall, not correctness.

**The general lesson, worth repeating for every future batch**: don't trust a sub-agent's own "should work" framing on an untested interactive flow — verify it live, and when you do, verify the *content* is right, not just that no exception was raised and the URL looks plausible. A fetcher that returns 0 jobs is an obvious, loud failure. A fetcher that returns 10 wrong-but-plausible-shaped jobs is not — it would have shipped silently and mislabeled non-India postings as India-based in real user-facing alerts.

**ICICI Bank** — not a third-party ATS at all: a fully in-house-built React SPA (`careers.icici.bank.in`) whose entire API (`CareerApplicantApi`) encrypts every request and response body with AES-128-CBC (key literal recovered directly from the SPA's own webpack bundle, IV appended as a plaintext suffix on the ciphertext string — a scheme reverse-engineered from the bundle's own `Object(o.g)`/`Object(o.f)` helpers and confirmed round-tripping live). This is the first fetcher in this repo requiring a crypto dependency — `pycryptodome>=3.20` added to `requirements.txt`. Keyword genuinely narrows server-side (confirmed: "manager" → 19/24 matches; every one of this repo's ten standard tech keywords → a genuine 0, same shape as a nonsense token's 0) — NOT registered in `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`. The zero-match result is a real fact about the live board: ICICI's own site defines a "Digital & Technology" category, but zero postings are currently routed through this public portal (~24 total live postings bank-wide, 100% retail-banking/ops/credit-risk/RM/CA/Probationary-Officer) — tech hiring evidently runs through other channels right now. Pagination gotcha: `PageNo` is a 0-indexed *multiplier*, not an additive counter — the API computes `OFFSET = PageNo * limit` server-side, discovered via an anomaly where `PageNo=1, limit=20` against a 24-row pool returned only the last 4 rows.

**Registry/config changes:**
- `_PIPELINE_DATA`: added `icicibank`, `uber`
- `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`: added `uber` (kept off `icicibank` — genuinely filters server-side)
- `_USES_PLAYWRIGHT`: added `uber`
- `config.yaml`: added `icicibank_search`, `uber_search`
- `requirements.txt`: added `pycryptodome>=3.20`
- Test count: 218 → 220; README: 218 → 220 companies

All 171 tests pass; `run_all.py --validate` confirms clean wiring for all 220 companies. This closes out Wave 15 in full — the FAANG gap is closed (Apple, Netflix, plus Amazon/Google/Meta/Microsoft already present), India's major private banks now have a foothold (HDFC, Axis, Kotak, ICICI), and a batch of dev-tool/cloud unicorns (Stripe, Snowflake, Databricks, MongoDB, Cloudflare, GitLab, Rippling, Uber, Canva, Workday) rounds out the wave.


## Air India integration completed (2026-09-07)

Completed the previously untracked Air India adapter and empty seen file, adding `airindia` to the registry and `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`, an `airindia_search` config section, and regression coverage. The local registry now contains **221 pipelines**. This entry records local integration and verification; deployment requires these changes to be committed and pushed.

**Data source and safeguards:** `careers.airindia.com/search/` uses SAP SuccessFactors J2W tile HTML, fetched with plain HTTP. Cache the full board once: keyword queries are noisy and `locationsearch=India` incorrectly returns zero. Live pagination returned **25 + 4 + 0** postings at `startrow=0/25/50`. Use the tablet location field because responsive variants differ; append India only for recognized Indian cities. Of 29 current postings, 28 have recognized Indian city locations and one says only `HO`; that ambiguous posting remains unlabelled and fails the shared India check. Missing result containers and request failures raise `RateLimitError`, retained for subsequent cache calls instead of silently becoming an empty success. The shared matcher's warning-only handling of search failures still applies.

**Live matching:** 29 fetched, **1 qualifying match**: `59182644`, “Senior Staff Engineer,” Gurugram, posted 2026-08-25, tagged `AI / ML / Python` via “generative ai” in its 4,141-character JD. The previously investigated “Engineer - Backend” (`59188944`) has a real 3,298-character Java/Spring Boot JD and no qualifying primary skill; SQL Server alone correctly does not qualify. “Agentic AI Architect” (`55349644`) has a substantive AI JD naming LangGraph/CrewAI but fails the existing shared title-family gate. That title-policy gap is documented, not changed as part of this company integration.

**Verification:** all **182 tests pass** and registry/config/fetcher validation succeeds for **221/221 pipelines**. Live checks used direct fetcher/matcher calls without notifications or production seen-state writes; `seen_jobs_airindia.json` remains empty so a later deployed run can alert eligible jobs. No browser or additional dependency is needed.

## Wave 16 (2026-09-07): Indian airlines/travel-tech, and major conglomerates' tech arms

User-prompted: "why don't we add companies such as Air India, etc." plus major Indian conglomerates with likely Mumbai/BLR/GGN/HYD postings. Two batches dispatched with an explicit instruction (learned the hard way from Uber in Wave 15) to live-test every fetcher's actual `fetch_jobs()`/`fetch_job_description()` output before reporting done, and to write a mocked unit test file per fetcher — every company below now has one, a new-but-good pattern this wave established (`tests/test_<slug>_fetcher.py`, no live network calls). The coordinator independently live-verified a sample from both batches against real output before merging, not just trusting the reports.

**Airlines & travel-tech (4 of 7 feasible — Air India already landed via a concurrent session, see the entry above):**
- **IndiGo** — IndiGo's own in-house middleware (`ms-careers-prod.goindigo.in`) wrapping a SAP SuccessFactors OData tenant underneath. Requires Playwright, but for an unusual reason: Akamai Bot Manager fingerprints headless **Chromium** specifically and serves a fake-HTTP-200 soft-fail page instead of a hard block — headless **Firefox** gets through cleanly. The API itself is then called via `page.evaluate(fetch(...))` from within that already-navigated Firefox context (must NOT set `credentials: 'include'` — breaks CORS on this tenant). 18 India jobs, keywords/location ignored server-side, descriptions inline.
- **Akasa Air** — Zoho Recruit (`akasaair.zohorecruit.in`), a different tenant on the same vendor as `yubi_fetcher.py`. Plain requests, no bot gating. 20 jobs, keywords ignored, descriptions inline.
- **MakeMyTrip** — a custom in-house API that itself proxies MMT's own Darwinbox tenant under the hood (revealed via the detail response's `applyUrl`). 37 jobs, keywords ignored, descriptions via a separate detail call. Gotcha: use `job_created_timestamp` for posting date, not `job_updated_timestamp` (bulk-touched daily by their sync, not a real signal).
- **ixigo** — SmartRecruiters (company id `ixigo`), same platform family as PhonePe/Nagarro, found by live-clicking a job card and capturing the resulting `jobs.smartrecruiters.com` URL rather than trusting ixigo's own `/api/openings` wrapper (which uses a divergent, undocumented ID scheme). 10 jobs, keywords genuinely narrow (loose/OR-based, but a nonsense token correctly returns 0).
- **SpiceJet, OYO, Yatra — genuinely infeasible, exhaustively checked.** SpiceJet's corporate careers page is purely static (a fraud warning, a cabin-crew walk-in calendar, a mailto link — zero API/ATS reference anywhere). OYO was checked against every ATS vendor pattern this repo knows (Greenhouse tenant dead, SmartRecruiters dead redirect, Darwinbox invalid subdomain, Zoho Recruit tenant exists but never populated beyond Zoho's own demo job, `oyo.com` redirects to an unrelated domain) — no live channel exists today. Yatra's careers pages are lead-capture resume-drop forms with hardcoded dropdowns; no job-list API exists behind them (confirmed live via Playwright — the `location=` URL param only pre-fills the form).

**Major Indian conglomerates' tech/digital arms (8 of 8 feasible):**
- **Reliance Industries** — its own legacy ASP.NET WebForms portal (`careers.ril.com/rilcareers`). Keyword search technically works but only through an encrypted-querystring redirect scheme confirmed live via Playwright and judged impractical to reverse-engineer for plain `requests` — instead, the fetcher found that the same results page serves the complete unfiltered board on a bare GET with no query string at all, so it fetches that directly (no Playwright needed in the end) and caches the whole ~16-job board once. DISTINCT from Jio Platforms, which already has its own separate tech-heavy fetcher.
- **Larsen & Toubro** (the parent conglomerate, distinct from already-onboarded LTTS/LTIMindtree) and **Aditya Birla Group** both run on **PeopleStrong "Candidate Portal"** — a new ATS vendor for this repo. `searchString` is a genuine case-insensitive literal-substring match, notably *narrower* than most tenants here (e.g. "senior software engineer" returns 0 even though "senior engineer" and "software engineer" separately return real results) — a good reminder that "keyword genuinely narrows" doesn't always mean phrase-AND semantics. Aditya Birla's own site wraps the same PeopleStrong tenant behind a first-party Next.js API requiring a static bearer token baked into the JS bundle; a bad/expired token comes back as HTTP 200 with a JSON `{"status":401,...}` body rather than a real 401, so the fetcher checks the body explicitly.
- **Vedanta** and **Bharti Airtel** both run on **Darwinbox** (different SPA tiers — Vedanta on the older "candidate" tier like Zomato/Sonata, Airtel on the newer "candidatev2" tier like Darwinbox's own board) but **neither is Cloudflare-gated**, unlike Sonata's and Darwinbox's own tenants — plain `requests` works for both, no Playwright needed. A useful reminder that bot-gating is a per-tenant decision on Darwinbox, not a platform-wide default.
- **Mahindra & Mahindra** (the parent auto/tractor conglomerate, distinct from already-onboarded Tech Mahindra) runs SAP SuccessFactors J2W classic. **Real, load-bearing gotcha found and fixed**: this tenant does not return an empty page once a keyword's true results are exhausted — it silently swaps in "the 25 most recent jobs" fallback content instead, under a label containing "There are currently no open positions matching...", and this fires *mid-pagination* too (not just for nonsense tokens) the moment a real keyword's own result count is exceeded. A naive "stop on empty rows" pagination guard would have silently ingested this wrong fallback data as real matches. The fetcher explicitly detects the fallback label text on every page and treats it as zero results.
- **JSW Group** runs TurboHire — the same vendor already onboarded for Flipkart, but a different tenant, and a good warning against assuming shared-vendor conventions transfer: Flipkart's big company-wide pool lives at `pageType=1`, but probing JSW's tenant directly found `pageType=0` is actually its largest pool (58 jobs) while `pageType=1` is one of its smallest (14) — re-verified live per tenant by capturing the real "View All Jobs" button's network request rather than assumed from precedent.
- **ITC Limited** runs Zoho Recruit — the same product as `zoho_fetcher.py` (Zoho Corp's own board), a different tenant. A live-testing catch worth naming explicitly: the agent's own mocked unit test caught a real bug before it shipped — the location normalizer was unconditionally force-appending ", India" to *any* non-India country string, which would have mislabeled a hypothetical Oman/UAE posting as India-based; fixed to only default when the `Country` field is genuinely absent, not merely non-India.

**Registry/config changes:**
- `_PIPELINE_DATA`: added `adityabirla`, `airtel`, `akasaair`, `indigo`, `itc`, `ixigo`, `jsw`, `larsentoubro`, `mahindra`, `makemytrip`, `reliance`, `vedanta`
- `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`: added `airindia`(from the concurrent session), `airtel`, `akasaair`, `indigo`, `itc`, `jsw`, `makemytrip`, `reliance`, `vedanta` (kept off `larsentoubro`, `adityabirla`, `mahindra` — all three genuinely filter server-side)
- `_INLINE_DESCRIPTIONS`: added `airtel`, `akasaair`, `indigo` (kept off `adityabirla`/`itc` despite serving most descriptions from cache — both have a real network-call fallback path for cache misses, so neither is unconditionally inline)
- `_USES_PLAYWRIGHT`: added `indigo`
- `config.yaml`: added a `_search` block for each of the 12 new companies
- Test count: 221 → 233; README: 221 → 233 companies
- 12 new `tests/test_<slug>_fetcher.py` files (mocked, no live network calls) covering pagination/dedup/location-normalization/failure-handling

All tests pass (327 total); `run_all.py --validate` confirms clean wiring for all 233 companies. ## Wave 17 (2026-09-08): Clearwater Analytics, Guidewire, Verisk, FICO — Insurance/BFSI Software Vendors, Plus One Genuine Skip

Four companies dispatched in parallel (each an independent agent, briefed to read this playbook first and live-verify before reporting done), all landing on **Workday or Oracle HCM CE with the standard India-facet-broken or India-facet-missing gotchas this repo already knows how to handle**:

- **Clearwater Analytics** — Workday, tenant `clearwateranalytics`, site `Clearwater_Analytics_Careers`. No country-level facet at all on this tenant (unlike most Workday tenants here); India scoped via 3 hardcoded office WIDs (Bengaluru/Mumbai/Noida). `searchText` genuinely narrows server-side but is deliberately ignored — the whole India-office pool is small (~51 jobs), so the fetcher caches it in full each pass and lets matcher.py's own filters do the real work (`_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`). `limit` caps at 20 (>20 → HTTP 400, the familiar Nvidia/Walmart/Northern Trust ceiling). No Chennai/Pune/Tamil Nadu office exists on this tenant at all. Live: 51 India jobs, 0 leakage.
- **Guidewire Software** — Workday, tenant `guidewire`, site `External`, hosted at `guidewire.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com`. Standard cross-tenant India `locationCountry` WID (`c4f78be1a8f14da0ab49ce1162348a5e`, same GUID reused by Fidelity/Wells Fargo/Citi/Northern Trust/MUFG/Shell) works cleanly here. All live India postings are in Bangalore. Live: 44 of 141 global jobs are India, 0 leakage; sample titles include "AI Engineer" (JD names LangChain/LangGraph/RAG/vector DB) and "Staff Software Engineer - Cloud Platform".
- **Verisk Analytics** — Oracle HCM CE, tenant `fa-ewmy-saasfaprod1.fa.ocs.oraclecloud.com`, site `CX_1` — same platform family as Chubb/Amex/BNY Mellon, a different tenant/site/facet (India facet ID `300000000455977`). New quirk in this family: a blank keyword combined with the India facet returns `TotalJobsCount: 0` (same failure shape as Wells Fargo's `limit=0` bug) — fetcher falls back to `"engineer"` when the caller passes a blank keyword. Live: 6-12 India jobs depending on keyword, all Hyderabad or bare "India", 0 leakage.
- **FICO** — Workday, tenant `fico`, site `External`, hosted at `fico.wd1.myworkdayjobs.com`. This tenant's `locationCountry` facet is present in job detail JSON but **broken/ignored at search time** (applying the standard India WID returns the full 88-job global pool, not filtered results) — same failure class as Micron/Verizon/Lowe's. Worked around with the tenant's city-level `locations` facet instead (3 India WIDs: Bangalore/Mumbai/Work from Home India), cross-verified against an independent `searchText="India"` probe returning an identical 16-job set. Live: 16 India jobs — Bangalore (13), Mumbai (2), Work From Home India (1) — zero Chennai/Pune/Tamil Nadu despite FICO having offices in those excluded cities generally; current live postings simply aren't posted there right now.

**Genuine skip: Intellect Design Arena.** India-HQ'd BFSI product company, so a real India pipeline was expected — investigation instead found the company's own official "Apply Now" funnel (`https://cloud.myadrenalin.com/CandidateMAX/#/?CompanyID=INTELLECT`) is Adrenalin eSystems' "CandidateMAX" product, a new ATS vendor for this repo. Confirmed the tenant is genuinely live and correctly identified (184 real office locations including several India cities, 122 position categories, 329 skills returned by the portal's own config endpoint) but the public vacancy-search call (`POST CPVacancyDetails/GetVacancyInformationWithoutToken`, `{"CompanyID":"INTELLECT","Flag":"OP"}`) returns an empty `VacancyInformation` array — reproduced identically via raw HTTP replay and via a real headless-Chromium render of the actual candidate page, which itself displays "We could not find jobs matching your search criteria." LinkedIn shows ~46 India postings for the company, so hiring is happening, just evidently not routed through this official portal right now — this repo has no LinkedIn-scraping precedent and other companies with the same "hiring elsewhere, empty on their own portal" shape (Optym, Dream11, Toptal/Turing, Trilogy) were flagged the same way rather than forced. No fetcher, config, or state file created; the verified request shape is recorded here in case Intellect republishes to this same portal later.

**Registry/config changes:**
- `_PIPELINE_DATA`: added `clearwater`, `fico`, `guidewire`, `verisk`

## Supplemental batch (2026-09-08): Eightfold.ai and Sprinklr

**Eightfold.ai** (the AI talent-platform vendor's own hiring pipeline, not one of the many other companies in this repo that merely *use* Eightfold as their ATS vendor) — runs on its own Eightfold Talent Platform tenant, `app.eightfold.ai/careers` (`domain=eightfold.ai`). Uses the same public PCSX search API as Microsoft/Morgan Stanley (`src/fetcher.py`) — this tenant does **not** have PCSX disabled the way HSBC's does, so no "related jobs" widget workaround was needed. Keyword search is a server-side no-op (a nonsense query token returned the exact same date-sorted position list, byte-for-byte, as a real one) — added to `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`. Confirmed live: 21 unique India postings (Bengaluru/Bangalore, Karnataka and Noida, Uttar Pradesh), 3 genuine `[AI / ML / Python]` matches (Staff Engineer, Lead Engineer, Staff Machine Learning Engineer) with real descriptions and apply links.

**Sprinklr** — Workday, tenant `sprinklr.wd1.myworkdayjobs.com`, site code `careers` (found via the public careers page's own outbound `myworkdayjobs.com` links, not guessed). India scoped server-side via the same cross-tenant `locationCountry` WID (`c4f78be1a8f14da0ab49ce1162348a5e`) already used by Wells Fargo/Citi. Two load-bearing findings during live verification:
- **`limit` over 20 returns a plain HTTP 400** (not a silent no-op like Shell) — clamped defensively in the fetcher, same discipline as every other Workday tenant here.
- **Pagination wraps around once `offset` reaches the true total** (confirmed live: `offset=32` on a 32-job India pool returns page 1 again instead of an empty list — same wraparound family as UBS/Nvidia/Walmart) — fixed with a module-level `_seen_job_ids` set so a wrapped page comes back fully empty and `matcher.py`'s `if not page: break` terminates pagination cleanly, rather than looping to `max_listings`.

32 confirmed live India postings (Gurgaon, Haryana and Bangalore, Karnataka — Sprinklr's real India hiring hubs). 0 matches against current listings — legitimate result, not a bug: the current India-facing openings are Java/NodeJS/Scala backend, implementation/managed-services, and design roles with no `.NET`/C#/hard-AI signal in their descriptions today; the near-miss log shows correct `[title family]`/`[exclude]`/`[broad-only]`/`[skill]` reasons for every filtered-out posting, no obvious false negatives.

**Registry/config changes:**
- `_PIPELINE_DATA`: added `eightfold`, `sprinklr`

- `seen_jobs_eightfold.json`, `seen_jobs_sprinklr.json` created as `[]`

## Wave 18 (2026-09-08): Fidelity International, D. E. Shaw India, Evernorth — Plus One Genuine Skip (Euroclear)

Three companies onboarded, one genuine skip, dispatched from a single agent briefed to read this playbook first and live-verify before reporting done:

- **Fidelity International (FIL)** — distinct legal entity from Fidelity Investments/FMR (already tracked as `fidelity`, untouched here). Workday, tenant `fil`, site `001` (`fil.wd3.myworkdayjobs.com`), found from the real careers page's own outbound link, not guessed. Standard cross-tenant India `locationCountry` WID (`c4f78be1a8f14da0ab49ce1162348a5e`) works; `searchText` genuinely narrows server-side (26 India jobs unfiltered → 5 for `.NET`). Two tenant-specific quirks not seen together elsewhere in this repo: (1) the search response has **no `locationsText`/`postedOn` fields at all** — only `title`, `externalPath`, and a `bulletFields` list (office name, "J##### Title (Open)", "Posting Date: DD/MM/YYYY" — day-first, confirmed against today's date); location and posting date are parsed out of `bulletFields` instead. (2) **The India facet is not primary-location-only** — it also matches jobs whose *primary* office is elsewhere (confirmed live: a "Cannon Street Office"/London-primary job and a "Dalian Office"/China-primary job both came back under the India facet, because India appeared only in their `additionalLocations`). Trusting the facet alone would have leaked non-India-primary jobs into the feed; fixed by gating on `bulletFields[0]` (the real primary office) against a known-India-city allowlist and dropping anything else, rather than passing ambiguous rows through for `matcher.py` to sort out. Also: `limit` > 20 → HTTP 400, and offset pagination wraps around past the true total (same wraparound family as UBS/Nvidia/Walmart/Sprinklr) — fixed with a module-level `_seen_job_ids` set. Live: 20 genuine India-primary jobs (Gurgaon + Bengaluru) after the leakage fix, 0 current matches (near-miss log shows correct title-family/exclude/skill reasons — legitimate result, not a bug, per the eClerx/ING/Arcesium precedent).

- **D. E. Shaw India** — distinct from Arcesium (an independent company since its 2015 spinoff, already tracked separately as `arcesium`, untouched here). The obvious guess, `deshaw.com/careers`, turned out to be the wrong site: its own Next.js `__NEXT_DATA__` payload is exclusively New York/Denver/London/Singapore (zero India) despite listing "India" as a location-filter option. Found the real pipeline via web search: D. E. Shaw runs a **separate country-specific domain**, `www.deshawindia.com/careers/work-with-us`, whose `__NEXT_DATA__.props.pageProps.regularJobs` (84 postings) is exclusively Hyderabad/Bengaluru/Gurugram — i.e. this domain *is* the India-scoped pipeline, no facet or filter logic needed. Same custom Next.js SSR shape as Arcesium's Greenhouse-adjacent pattern in spirit (whole page fetched and cached once, full description embedded server-side, no query-param search API) — registered in `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`. No posting-date field exists anywhere in this site's schema (unlike Arcesium's `updated_at`); `posting_date` is always `""`. Live: 84 India jobs, 2 confirmed matches (GenAI/Python-track "Software Engineer" roles), 0 leakage — a genuine Python/Go-heavy AI/ML shop with no .NET/C# signal observed, consistent with a quant-fund India dev office.

- **Evernorth** (Cigna's health services arm) — its own careers page (`jobs.cigna.com/evernorth`, 301s to `jobs.thecignagroup.com/evernorth`) is a Phenom People landing page whose embedded `phApp.ddo` is only a category-count summary, not a real search response — the actual ATS was found by reading that page's own `applyUrl` links, which resolve to plain Workday: tenant `cigna`, site `cignacareers` (`cigna.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com`). Evernorth has no separate Workday tenant of its own; it's one brand posted through Cigna Group's shared tenant, scoped down via `searchText="Evernorth"` (confirmed identical to `searchText="HIH"` — Evernorth's Hyderabad Innovation Hub, opened 2024) against the standard cross-tenant India WID (136 India jobs group-wide → 66 Evernorth-branded). Two gotchas: offset pagination wraps around past the true total (same family as Fidelity International above), and the API's own `total` field is unreliable mid-walk (reads 0 on some interior pages, 66 on others, for the identical query — a Verisk/Wells-Fargo-style quirk). Fixed by pinning the keyword server-side and caching the full pool via a dedup-based page walk that stops on the first page contributing no new job ID (survives both the wraparound and the bad `total` field) — registered in `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS` since the caller's own keyword is never applied (combining it with the fixed "Evernorth" term risked AND-narrowing real jobs out — confirmed live: adding "software engineer" on top dropped the pool from 66 to 46). Live: 65 unique India jobs (Hyderabad + one Bengaluru), 11 confirmed matches — genuine Python/GenAI/LangChain-track roles ("Machine Learning Associate Advisor", multiple "Software Engineering ... Advisor/Manager" with GenAI/RAG/vector-DB skills), 0 leakage.

**Genuine skip: Euroclear.** European post-trade settlement infrastructure firm — a real India GCC presence was plausible (LinkedIn shows ~19-144 "Euroclear" India listings depending on query) but investigation found the company's own official ATS tells a different story. Identified the real ATS from the careers page (`euroclear.com/careers`) → Oracle HCM CE, tenant `don.fa.em2.oraclecloud.com`, site `CX_1003` (a different tenant/site from every other Oracle HCM CE company already in this repo — Chubb/Amex/Verisk/etc. — not a copy-paste guess). Queried the live `recruitingCEJobRequisitions` API directly: **the tenant's own country-level facet lists only Poland, Belgium, UK, France, Netherlands, Malaysia, Singapore, Hong Kong, and Japan — no India entry at all**, across a 138-job global pool. A direct `keyword="India"` probe against the same API returned exactly 1 result, a "Test Lead" role based in **Poland** (the word "India" appearing incidentally in the description, not as a location). Cross-checked against `workLocationsFacet` (real physical offices: Krakow, Brussels, London, Paris, Amsterdam, Kuala Lumpur, Hong Kong, Tokyo — no Indian city) — confirms this isn't a broken/hidden facet, Euroclear genuinely has no India office in its own hiring system right now. The LinkedIn "India" listings for "Euroclear" likely come from staffing agencies or vendor postings referencing the client name, not Euroclear's own official pipeline — same "third-party noise vs. real employer ATS" shape flagged for other genuine skips in this repo (Intellect Design Arena, T. Rowe Price, Discover). No fetcher, config, or state file created.

**Registry/config changes:**
- `_PIPELINE_DATA`: added `fidelityinternational`, `deshaw`, `evernorth`
- `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`: added `deshaw`, `evernorth` (kept off `fidelityinternational` — genuinely filters server-side)
- `_INLINE_DESCRIPTIONS`: added `deshaw` (full description is embedded in the same SSR page as the listing, no separate detail call)

## Wave 19 (2026-09-08): Duck Creek Technologies, Sapiens International, Plus Two Genuine Skips (CVS Health, Temenos)

Four companies dispatched: insurance core-systems and healthcare/banking software vendors, live-verified before any code was written.

- **Duck Creek Technologies** — Workday, tenant `duckcreek`, site `duckcreekcareers`, hosted at `duckcreek.wd1.myworkdayjobs.com` (found via outbound job links on `www.duckcreek.com/careers/`). Tiny tenant (~23 total live reqs), no `locationCountry` facet at all, so the fetcher fetches the full pool per keyword and lets each job's own detail payload decide India membership — same conservative shape as Clearwater Analytics. `searchText` genuinely narrows server-side ("engineer" cut 23 → 13). New quirk: many postings are dual-located (e.g. "Mumbai, India" + "Bengaluru, India") and the search-result `locationsText` field just says `"2 Locations"` with no readable city text — naively trusting that string would fail matcher.py's literal `"india" in location.lower()` Layer 1 check. Fixed by resolving any `"N Locations"` posting through one extra detail-page GET (`location` + `additionalLocations` fields) and joining into a `"City, India; City2, India"` string — cheap given the tiny tenant size. All live India postings are Mumbai (some dual-posted with Bengaluru); no Chennai/Pune/Tamil Nadu/Chandigarh/Kochi postings exist on this tenant right now. Live: 10 India jobs (of 23 global), 0 leakage, includes genuine `[AI / ML / Python]`-flavored roles (a Principal Software Architect JD names LangChain/Pinecone/Weaviate/RAG).
- **Sapiens International** — classic (non-Unify) SAP SuccessFactors J2W tenant at `careers.sapiens.com` (found via outbound `career5.successfactors.eu`/`/platform/js/j2w/...` asset references on the public careers page) — server-rendered `<tr class="data-row">` HTML, no session priming or CSRF token needed, matching the playbook's documented classic-J2W shape exactly (`<span class="jobdescription">` for description, `<meta itemprop="datePosted">` for date). Two load-bearing findings:
  - The site's `title` query param does a **literal-substring match, not an OR-token match** — a multi-word configured keyword like `"software engineer"` returns zero rows against real titles like "Senior AI Engineer" or "DevOps Architect" that never contain that exact phrase. Registered in `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`; the fetcher always leaves `title` blank and scopes purely via the separate `locationsearch` param (confirmed more precise than stuffing the term into the general `q` full-text field, which also loosely matched some non-India rows).
  - **The site renders location as `"City, IN"` (2-letter ISO country code), not the word "India"** — matcher.py's Layer 1 check is a literal `"india" in location.lower()` substring test, so every job would have silently failed Layer 1 with no near-miss log line at all (that check has no logging branch) had this gone unnoticed. Fixed with a small `_normalize_location` regex substitution (`", IN"` suffix → `", India"`) inside the fetcher, confirmed via an end-to-end dry run through `run_company_pipeline` with a stubbed notifier that near-miss lines started appearing correctly (`[title family]`/`[exclude]`/`[skill]`) only after the fix.
  - `startrow` is a row-offset, not a page number (confirmed `startrow=15` on a 24-row result returns rows 15-23), but the site's own fixed page size (15) doesn't divide evenly into matcher.py's 20-row stepping — `fetch_jobs` loops internally over the site's own smaller pages until it assembles a full contiguous `num`-sized page, so no row is ever silently skipped.
  Live: 22 India jobs (of 78 global), all Bangalore, 0 leakage; 0 current matches is a legitimate result (the one title-family-eligible role, "Senior AI Engineer", has a placeholder `"TA to be update"` description on Sapiens' own site right now — a real data-quality gap on their end, not a fetcher bug — confirmed via the near-miss log's `[skill] desc=15 chars` line).

**Genuine skip: CVS Health.** US healthcare/retail company; investigated rather than assumed, per instructions. Official careers site is Workday, tenant `cvshealth`, site `CVS_Health_Careers` — direct API queries confirm 18,930 total live reqs but **zero `locationCountry`/`locationMainGroup` facet value anywhere in the country namespace**, and direct `searchText` probes for `"Hyderabad"`, `"Bangalore"`, and `"Bengaluru"` each return `total: 0`; a `searchText="India"` probe returns 187 results that are entirely false-positive matches on "Indianapolis"/"IN - Indianapolis" (Indiana, the US state), not the country. No separate India-specific Workday tenant or subdomain exists. Despite public reporting of a growing India GCC/tech-center presence, the company's own live official hiring portal shows no real India req pipeline today. No fetcher, config, or state file created.

**Genuine skip: Temenos.** Global core-banking software vendor with a widely-reported large India (Bangalore/Chennai) engineering presence per LinkedIn/Naukri (100+ listings each) — investigation instead found the company's only official portal, Workday tenant `temenos` / site `Temenoscareers` (`temenos.wd103.myworkdayjobs.com`, the only host referenced anywhere on `temenos.com/about-us/careers/`), currently carries just **18 total live reqs globally**, entirely sales/finance/leadership roles (Business Development Manager, Account Manager, Financial Controller, etc.) with zero engineering titles and zero India locations — confirmed via multiple direct keyword probes (`""`, `"engineer"`, `"developer"`, `"India"`, `"Bangalore"`) against the live CXS API, and by paging through the complete 18-row pool by hand. No alternate site ID resolves under the same tenant (`External`/`TemenosIndia`/`GlobalCareers`/`Careers` all 404). Same "third-party aggregators show hiring activity the company's own live official portal does not currently reflect" shape as Intellect Design Arena/Optym/Dream11/Toptal-Turing/Trilogy — flagged rather than forced. No fetcher, config, or state file created.

**Registry/config changes:**
- `_PIPELINE_DATA`: added `duckcreek`, `sapiens`
- `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`: added `sapiens` (kept off `duckcreek` — genuinely filters server-side, confirmed "engineer" narrowed the pool from 23 to 13)
- `_SUPPORTS_LOCATION`: added `sapiens` (genuine dedicated `locationsearch` param)
- `config.yaml`: added `duckcreek_search`, `sapiens_search`
- `seen_jobs_duckcreek.json`, `seen_jobs_sapiens.json` created as `[]`
- Test count: registry-count assertion bumped 244 → 246; README: 244 → 246 companies
- 2 new `tests/test_<slug>_fetcher.py` files (mocked, no live network calls)

All 434 tests pass; `python src/run_all.py --validate --companies duckcreek sapiens` confirms clean wiring. Both fetchers' `fetch_jobs()`/`fetch_job_description()` were independently live-verified against real network calls, and the full `matcher.py`/near-miss/`run_company_pipeline` path was exercised end-to-end with a temporary seen-state file and a stubbed notifier — no real alerts sent, no seen-state files advanced.

## Wave 20 (2026-09-08): Indian fintechs and conversational-AI platforms — Pine Labs, Angel One, Navi, Uniphore, Yellow.ai

Five India-headquartered companies dispatched: two fintechs landed real, credible-looking careers portals that turned out to genuinely have almost no listings when investigated live; one fintech (Navi) and one AI platform (Uniphore) onboarded cleanly; one AI platform (Yellow.ai) turned out to have the same "portal is real but empty" shape as Pine Labs.

**Navi** — the site's own client JS (`navi.com/careers/jobs`) calls a first-party proxy, `GET https://navi.com/api/careers/jobs`, which wraps a TurboHire tenant (`navi.turbohire.co` — same ATS vendor as Flipkart/JSW elsewhere in this repo, reached here through Navi's own domain instead of directly). Plain `requests.get()`, no auth/cookies, no bot gating. `?search=` exists as a param but a real keyword and a nonsense token both return the identical `FilteredCount: 36` — the whole (small) board is returned regardless, so keyword is ignored server-side (added to `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`) and cached once per process. `JobDescriptionV2` is full inline HTML — no separate detail call needed. Live: 36 total postings, all Bangalore/Bengaluru or Delhi (no Pune/Chennai/Tamil Nadu/Kochi/Chandigarh observed). Two genuine engineering titles ("SDE III - Security", "SDE III - Mobile Security") correctly reach the shared matcher; both filtered out today as `[broad-only]` (mention Python but no primary-skill term) — a legitimate zero-match result, not a fetcher bug.

**Uniphore** — Workday. Two candidate hosts exist; `uniphore.wd1.myworkdayjobs.com` returns HTTP 422 for every site/tenant combination tried, while `uniphore.wd503.myworkdayjobs.com` (site `Uniphore`) answers cleanly — a reminder to probe rather than trust the first Workday host a search result surfaces. **New gotcha for this tenant family**: the standard cross-tenant `locationCountry` India WID (`c4f78be1a8f14da0ab49ce1162348a5e`) is broken here — applying it returns the full 39-job global pool unfiltered, same failure class as Micron/Verizon/Lowe's/FICO. Worked around with the tenant's city-level `locations` facet instead (Bangalore + Chennai WIDs), cross-verified live (4 India jobs vs. 39 unfiltered). **Second gotcha, also new**: the list response's `locationsText` is frequently the unhelpful `"2 Locations"` placeholder (Maersk-style) — every current India posting is dual-listed "India - Bangalore" + "India - Chennai" as alternate options. Naively keeping that placeholder would either hide the real city or falsely exclude a Bangalore-primary role merely because Chennai is listed as an alternate. Fixed by fetching each job's detail record (cheap — only ~4 India jobs) and using its `location` field, the single authoritative *primary* site (confirmed live: all 4 current postings are `location: "India - Bangalore"` with Chennai only in `additionalLocations`) — a genuinely Chennai-primary posting would still resolve to Chennai and get correctly excluded. Detail responses carry full `jobDescription` HTML inline, cached during the same call so `fetch_job_description` is normally a free cache hit. `searchText` genuinely narrows results server-side (confirmed: "security" → 3 of 4, a nonsense token → 0) — not added to `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`. Live: 4 India postings (all Bangalore), 1 genuine `[AI / ML / Python]` match today — "Senior Staff Software Engineer (SDET)", whose JD explicitly names LangChain and Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) — exercised end-to-end through `matcher.py`/`notifier.format_message` with a stubbed notifier and temp seen-state file; alert formatting and tagging confirmed correct, no real alert sent, no seen-state advanced.

**Genuine skips — three companies, each independently confirmed via live network capture, not guessed:**
- **Pine Labs** — Darwinbox, tenant `pinelabs` (Cloudflare-gated `candidatev2` tier, same shape as Perfios — requires Playwright/Firefox; a plain `requests.get()` to the API gets a bare 403 from Cloudflare). The tenant's own `getCompanyConfig` response carries an authoritative `allJobsCount: 3` field, and the rendered board (confirmed via Playwright) lists exactly those 3 postings: two "Assistant Manager" roles and one "Sales Manager" role — zero engineering. Not a fetcher limitation; the company's official Darwinbox portal itself is nearly empty right now despite Pine Labs clearly hiring engineers elsewhere (LinkedIn shows 20+ India listings) — same "hiring elsewhere, empty on their own portal" shape as Intellect Design Arena (Wave 17).
- **Yellow.ai** — Zoho Recruit, tenant `yellow.zohorecruit.in`, reached through a first-party proxy at `careers.yellow.ai/recruit/v2/public/Job_Openings?pagename=Careers&source=CareerSite` (found via live Playwright network capture of `yellow.ai/career/`). The API and the fully-rendered page agree: exactly 1 posting exists on the whole tenant today, a "GTM recruiter" role already marked **"Position filled"** (closed) on the page itself. Verified there's no working pagination/extra-params trick (`&limit=`/`&from=` both hit `EXTRA_PARAM_FOUND` — the endpoint is strict) and cross-checked the full HTML-embedded jobs blob at `careers.yellow.ai/jobs/Careers` independently — same single closed job. Real engineering hiring clearly exists (Instahyre/Naukri list SDE roles) but isn't routed through this official portal right now.
- **Angel One** — investigated extensively but found genuinely infeasible today, a different failure shape from the other two. `angelone.in/careers` (and its `careers.angelone.in` alias, which 301s to the same page) is a Next.js app whose "Open Positions" section renders a Departments dropdown with **zero options** and an empty results container, and — confirmed via full unfiltered Playwright network capture (all resource types, 10s wait, geo-spoofed `X-Forwarded-For`/`CF-IPCountry: IN` headers, direct interaction with the search box) — the page never issues **any** XHR/fetch call that could plausibly be a jobs API; only ad-tech/analytics traffic fires. The initial server-rendered HTML (checked via its Next.js RSC `self.__next_f.push` stream) also contains no embedded job data. Job-detail URLs surfaced by web search (e.g. `angelone.in/careers/sde-2-backend-6001`) now 404. This reads as a genuinely broken/disabled client-side widget on Angel One's own site today, not a hidden API this repo's conventions failed to find — no fetcher, config, or state file created.

**Registry/config changes:**
- `_PIPELINE_DATA`: added `navi`, `uniphore`
- `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`: added `navi` (kept off `uniphore` — genuinely filters server-side, confirmed "security" narrowed 4→3 and a nonsense token → 0)
- `_INLINE_DESCRIPTIONS`: added `navi` (kept off `uniphore` — has a real network-call fallback path for cache misses, same discipline as Aditya Birla/ITC in Wave 16)
- `config.yaml`: added `navi_search`, `uniphore_search`
- `seen_jobs_navi.json`, `seen_jobs_uniphore.json` created as `[]`
- Test count bumped by 21 (registry-count assertion +2); README company count +2
- 2 new `tests/test_<slug>_fetcher.py` files (mocked, no live network calls)

`python src/run_all.py --validate --companies navi uniphore` confirms clean wiring; full local `pytest` suite passes.
