## Wave 21 (2026-09-08): Finastra, Newgen Software, TransUnion CIBIL

Three companies dispatched with a live-verify-before-reporting brief. All three had a real India pipeline; none were skipped.

- **Finastra** — Workday, tenant `finastra`, site `FINC` (`finastra.wd3.myworkdayjobs.com`), found via the careers page's own outbound `myworkdayjobs.com` link. This tenant has no `locationCountry` facet at all (probing the standard cross-tenant India WID returns HTTP 400), and `locationsText` omits the country entirely ("Pune", "Bengaluru") — same shape as Invesco. India detected client-side via city/state tokens, `", India"` appended for the matcher; Pune/Chennai/etc. are NOT filtered by the fetcher itself so matcher.py's `exclude_locations` layer (which needs the city name present) can do that job. `searchText` genuinely narrows server-side. Live: 47 unique India jobs (mostly Bengaluru + Pune); 0 current matches — Bengaluru pool is architects/BAs/tax/product roles with no `.NET`/AI hard-skill signal today (`Angular`/`TypeScript`-only postings correctly rejected as `[broad-only]`), and Pune carries most of the actual "Software Engineer" titles but is an excluded city.
- **Newgen Software** — investigated the company's own careers portal from scratch (India-HQ'd BPM/low-code vendor, so a real pipeline was expected). `newgensoft.com/company/careers/` links to `omnirecruit.newgen.co.in/IShareReferral/CareerPortal.aspx` — an in-house ASP.NET Web Forms ATS ("OmniRecruit"), not a third-party vendor. No JSON API; the entire job list (including full description text, both the "...More" and "...Less" toggle variants) is server-rendered directly into repeated `<div id="omnirecruit_referFadeEffects">` panels. The page's own `__doPostBack` `DataPager` (`Total Records :22`) did not actually advance pages when replayed (kept re-serving page 1) — worked around by discovering the portal's `txtSearchKeyWord` field DOES filter server-side (confirmed: "developer" narrows 10→2 visible), so the full ~22-job board was recovered by unioning ~20 overlapping keyword probes by job ID, then cached once per process (`_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`, `_INLINE_DESCRIPTIONS`) rather than re-probed per configured keyword. Live: 22 unique India jobs (Noida/Mumbai/Chennai) — sales/support/admin/BA-heavy with a handful of engineering-adjacent roles (Oracle DBA/WebLogic admin, AWS Cloud Support, Cyber Security, one Java-only "Software Engineer/Senior Software Engineer"); 0 current matches, a legitimate small-board result (same shape as Sprinklr/Juspay) — the location badge on that one Java posting says "Mumbai, India" while its own JD body says "Location: PUNE", a data-quality mislabel on Newgen's side noted in the fetcher docstring but not fixable from here.
- **TransUnion CIBIL** — transunioncibil.com's own site is bot-protected (plain `requests` gets HTTP 403 on every page). Probing common Workday tenant slugs found `transunion.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com/TransUnion` responds live — i.e. the India credit-bureau joint venture shares its parent's Workday tenant rather than running a separate ATS (confirmed: this tenant's own `locationsText` includes `"Mumbai - One World Center"`, CIBIL's real Mumbai HQ address). Scoped to India only via the standard cross-tenant `locationCountry` WID (`c4f78be1a8f14da0ab49ce1162348a5e`, same GUID as Wells Fargo/Citi/Fidelity/Sprinklr/Guidewire). Slug deliberately `transunioncibil`, not `transunion`, so a future separate global-TransUnion integration can't collide. `searchText` genuinely narrows server-side. Live: 32 India jobs (Bengaluru/Mumbai/Chennai/Pune); includes two genuine `Lead/Sr Developer, C# .NET & APIs` postings and several GenAI-titled roles, but both real `.NET` postings and most developer titles currently sit in Pune (excluded) — 0 current matches, correctly explained by near-miss log entries (`[title family]`, `[skill]`, `[broad-only]`) with no false positives; a Java-only role and an Azure-only role were both correctly rejected for lacking hard `.NET`/AI signal.

**Registry/config changes:**
- `_PIPELINE_DATA`: added `finastra`, `newgen`, `transunioncibil`
- `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`: added `newgen` (kept off `finastra`/`transunioncibil` — both genuinely filter server-side)
- `_INLINE_DESCRIPTIONS`: added `newgen` (full description text already present in the search-result HTML, no separate detail fetch)
- `config.yaml`: added `finastra_search`, `newgen_search`, `transunioncibil_search`
- `seen_jobs_finastra.json`, `seen_jobs_newgen.json`, `seen_jobs_transunioncibil.json` created as `[]`
- Test count: 251 → 254 (registry count-guard bumped); README: 251 → 254 companies
- 3 new `tests/test_<slug>_fetcher.py` files (mocked, no live network calls)

All 436 tests pass; `python src/run_all.py --validate --companies finastra newgen transunioncibil` confirms clean wiring. Every new fetcher's actual `fetch_jobs()`/`fetch_job_description()` output was independently live-verified against real network calls, and the full `matcher.py` filter pipeline (title family → exclude → skills → tags) was exercised end-to-end per company with a temporary seen-state file and a stubbed notifier — no real alerts sent, no tracked seen-state files advanced.

## Wave 22 (2026-09-08): 20 verified additions across BFSI, insurance, AI, enterprise software, and GCCs

Every agent reread this playbook before source discovery. A company was added only after its official employer source returned real India jobs and its list, pagination, location, description, date, URL, and matcher behavior were exercised. This wave takes the registry from 254 to 274.

**Greenhouse/Ashby/Lever full-board sources:**
- **AQR** — dedicated official Greenhouse board `india`; 6 India jobs live.
- **WorldQuant** — Greenhouse board `worldquant`; 5 India jobs out of 101 global.
- **Tower Research Capital** — official careers page embeds Greenhouse board `towerresearchcapital`; 13 India jobs out of 85 global. Its nonstandard `?gh_jid=` links require extracting the job ID rather than assuming the usual path form.
- **Observe.AI** — official Greenhouse board `observeai`; 9 Bengaluru jobs in the final live check.
- **Sarvam AI** — official Ashby posting API; 63 India jobs and one current strict match, `Backend Engineer, Chanakya`.
- **Dun & Bradstreet** — official Lever board `dnb`; full descriptions are inline. These six boards are cached and filtered locally, so their registry metadata deliberately marks keyword search ignored and descriptions inline where applicable.

**Workday and Oracle HCM sources:**
- **Apex Group** — Workday `theapexgroup.wd3` / `apexgroupcareers`; the standard India country WID returns 365 India jobs. Resolve `N Locations` through detail data so an excluded Pune primary location cannot leak through.
- **Prudential** — official Workday; live `engineer` search returned 9 India jobs and complete detail records.
- **Sun Life** — Workday `sunlife.wd3` / `Experienced`; standard India country WID returned 3 jobs.
- **Equifax** — Workday `equifax.wd5` / `External`; country facet reported 7 but only 5 were India-primary after rejecting additional-location leakage.
- **Murex** — Workday `murex.wd3` / `MurexCareerPage1`; tenant-specific Mumbai location WID returned 3 India jobs out of 60 global.
- **Wolters Kluwer** — Workday `wk.wd3` / `External`; no reliable country facet, so each search result is gated by its `IND` location code before normalization.
- **Emerson** — Oracle HCM CE site `CX_1` at `hdjq.fa.us2.oraclecloud.com`; India facet ID `300000000228786` returned 152 jobs out of 1,019 global.

**Other official sources:**
- **Citco** — Oracle HCM CE `CX_1`; India facet ID `300000000431886` returned 98 India jobs, and `software engineer` returned one current listing with a complete description.
- **SMBC** — SAP SuccessFactors J2W at `careerasia.smbc.co.jp/SMBC`; both keyword and `locationsearch` are genuine. Resolve `customfield1` from detail because the list says only `India`, preserving Chennai exclusion.
- **Nucleus Software** — official Zoho Recruit feed; 26 published India jobs from 37 records, descriptions inline, and one current strict match: `AI/ML Engineer` in Noida.
- **Progress Software** — official server-rendered careers board; 7 India jobs. Query parameters do not constrain its rows, so cache the small board and filter locally.
- **Quest Software** — official `careers-quest` iCIMS tenant; 2 India jobs from roughly 40 global. The iframe view exposes the complete SSR rows; preserve `APJ-IN` codes and normalize them to India.
- **Bosch** — official SmartRecruiters tenant `BoschGroup`; structured `country=in` returned 561 jobs and keyword filtering genuinely narrows.
- **Nielsen** — official SmartRecruiters tenant `TheNielsenCompany`; 61 India roles across Bengaluru, Hyderabad, and Mumbai. This is Nielsen, not NielsenIQ.

**Reusable lessons:**
- A country facet can include jobs where India is only an additional location. Treat the detail record's primary location as authoritative when the list says `N Locations` or the facet count exceeds India-primary results.
- Two-letter `IN` is ambiguous. Accept it only inside a source-specific country or region code such as `APJ-IN`; never treat arbitrary `IN` text as India because US portals use it for Indiana.
- An official careers page may expose a complete embedded dataset even when its visible filter UI is misleading. Cache the verified small board, then let the shared matcher perform title and skill filtering.
- Opaque Greenhouse tokens can replace old branded tokens after a migration. Verify the current public board API; stale indexed `gh_jid` pages are not evidence of a live India pipeline.
- Keep distinct corporate identities distinct: Nielsen vs NielsenIQ, Quest Software vs Quest Diagnostics/Quest Global, and TransUnion CIBIL vs a future global TransUnion integration.

**Validation and state safety:** 20 company-owned fetchers, 20 empty seen-state files, and focused source/contract tests were added. Shared registry/config wiring was validated without notifications; live checks used direct fetcher and matcher paths, never the tracked state files.

---

## False-positive fix: "Nice to Have" AI/ML terms no longer count as a match (2026-09-09)

**User-reported.** Citi sent two alerts (job IDs 26990861, 26990878 — same JD, two real Workday requisitions, correctly not a dedup bug) for "Senior Java Backend Developer- Assistant Vice President", tagged `[AI / ML / Python]`. Fetched the live description directly: the role is Core Java/Spring Boot/Microservices with zero .NET, and its only AI/ML mention was under an explicit **"Nice to Have"** heading — "Exposure to GenAI applications, LLM-powered solutions, RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation), MCP...". That phrase matched `primary_skills["AI / ML / Python"]` → `"retrieval augmented generation"`, and both Layer 3 (`matcher.py`) and Citi's Layer 4 `require_tech_in_description` were plain substring checks over the *entire* description — no distinction between a required skill and an explicitly-optional one.

**Fix:** added `matcher._strip_optional_sections()` — truncates the description at the first occurrence of an unambiguous "not required" heading (`nice to have`, `good to have`, `bonus points`/`bonus if`, `would be a plus`, `a plus if`, `optional skills`) before primary_skills/`require_tech_in_description` matching. Applied in `find_matching_jobs` (primary_skills + tag derivation) and in `run_company.py`'s Layer 4 filter. The broad `skills` gate still reads the full, untruncated description — this only tightens the *narrow*, track-defining checks.

**Deliberately not touched: "Preferred Qualifications."** Several real AI/.NET roles across this repo genuinely gate their core stack behind a "Preferred Qualifications" heading rather than "Required" — cutting there would trade this false positive for new false negatives on those companies. Only headings that are unambiguous ("nice to have", "bonus", etc.) are treated as optional.

Regression guards: `test_strip_optional_sections_*` (unit tests for the helper, including a case proving "Preferred Qualifications" content is preserved) and `test_find_matching_jobs_ignores_ai_term_in_nice_to_have_section` (a Citi-shaped fixture — Java/Spring Boot title, RAG mentioned only under "Nice to Have" — asserting it produces no match at all) in `tests/test_matcher.py`. Full suite (507 tests) passes after the change. This is a shared-matcher fix, so it applies to all 274 companies, not just Citi.

## Priceline (Booking Holdings) added (2026-09-13)

Priceline's branded careers site (`careers.priceline.com`) is a plain WordPress/WP Engine site with its own custom `job` post type — server-rendered listing/detail pages, `wp-json` REST API blocked (401), so scraping the WordPress HTML directly was the fallback plan. But every "Apply Now" link on those detail pages resolves to plain Workday (`priceline.wd1.myworkdayjobs.com`, tenant `priceline`, site `Priceline`), found by reading the real link destination rather than guessing — same "a branded front end can hide the real ATS" lesson as GE Aerospace/Boeing/Disney. Called the Workday CXS API directly instead of scraping WordPress: same underlying data, but as clean JSON with server-side `searchText` narrowing (confirmed "" → 65 total, "engineer" → 36) and a real detail payload for descriptions/dates.

Small tenant: 65 total live reqs globally, only 19 in India — all Mumbai (confirmed via the WordPress site's own `_job_location` facet list, which has no other Indian city option). No `locationCountry` facet exists (`/facets` endpoint 405s), so the fetcher fetches the full pool per keyword and filters India client-side via `locationsText`; `limit` > 20 returns HTTP 400 (same family as Northern Trust/Duck Creek) so `num` is hard-capped at 20, matching matcher.py's own pagination step. A handful of postings show as ambiguous "N Locations" text with no readable city — resolved via one extra detail-page GET per posting (cheap on a 65-job tenant); every one checked resolves to Norwalk/CT + New York/Toronto combinations, never Mumbai, but the fetcher doesn't assume that will always hold. `searchText` genuinely narrows server-side, so `priceline` is NOT in `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS` (same precedent as Duck Creek).

Live check: 19 unique Mumbai jobs fetched (Senior Software Engineer - Backend/iOS, Software Engineer ×3, SDET, Site Reliability Engineer, Staff Data Architect, Data Scientist, etc.), 0 leakage (no Pune/Kochi/Chandigarh/Chennai/Tamil Nadu — Mumbai is the only India office). 0 current `primary_skills` matches — every technology-track JD checked is Java/Spring/GCP/Kubernetes or NLP/statistical-ML flavored, with no `.NET`/`C#` and no hard `AI / ML / Python` term (the literal "RAG" substring appeared only inside "cove**rag**e"/"leve**rag**e" — correctly not a match, confirming the existing bare-"RAG" ban is doing its job here). Same legitimate "0 matches today" outcome as Sapiens/eClerx — a real, working pipeline just hasn't posted a qualifying role yet.

Registry/config changes: `_PIPELINE_DATA` gained `priceline` (source: "Priceline (Booking Holdings)", `require_tech_in_description` NOT enabled); `config.yaml` gained `priceline_search`; `seen_jobs_priceline.json` created as `[]`. Not added to `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`, `_SUPPORTS_LOCATION`, `_INLINE_DESCRIPTIONS`, `_NEWEST_FIRST`, or `_USES_PLAYWRIGHT` — none of those behaviors were observed. `python src/run_all.py --validate --companies priceline` confirms clean wiring; `fetch_jobs`/`fetch_job_description` were independently live-verified against real network calls.

## Booking.com added (2026-09-13)

`careers.booking.com`'s own "See all jobs" button leads to a *different* domain, `jobs.booking.com/booking/jobs` — a Jibe Careers Site Builder page (`app.jibecdn.com` assets, `jasession`/`rms-node` cookies) over iCIMS, the same ATS family already integrated at PepsiCo/Schneider Electric/HealthEdge/SITA/Gallagher/S&P Global Careers. Confirmed via direct API probes, not guessed: every job's own `ats_code` reads `"icims"` and every `apply_url` points at a `*-workingatbooking.icims.com` host. One wrinkle not seen at the sibling companies: the search UI's own path carries a `/booking/` brand prefix (this tenant is shared across Booking Holdings' portfolio — Agoda/Priceline/Kayak/OpenTable/Rentalcars all appear in the footer), but the actual JSON API has NO such prefix — `GET jobs.booking.com/api/jobs` works, the naive `jobs.booking.com/booking/api/jobs` guess 404s.

`location=India` genuinely filters server-side (32 jobs, matches the response's own `totalCount`); `keywords` also genuinely narrows server-side but is deliberately ignored (registered in `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`) since the whole India pool fits in one `limit=100` request (over-cap returns HTTP 422, same as SITA/Schneider Electric/HealthEdge); `offset` is silently broken (same shape as PepsiCo/HealthEdge/SITA) — cached once instead. Full description is embedded inline in the search response (`_INLINE_DESCRIPTIONS`), no separate detail fetch needed. `apply_url` 405s for plain HTTP (same protection class as HealthEdge/SITA), so alerts link to the public `jobs.booking.com/booking/jobs/{id}` page instead (confirmed rendering the correct title with no login wall; the singular `/booking/job/{id}` guess 404s).

Live-verified: 32 India postings, all Mumbai (1) or Bengaluru (31) — no Pune/Chennai/Kochi/Tamil Nadu leakage. Mostly Finance/Accounting/Tax/HR roles with a real engineering slice. One current genuine match: "Senior Full Stack Application Developer" (React/Angular/Node.js/Java/Python plus explicit LLM/Generative AI/RAG requirements), tagged `[AI / ML / Python]`. Titles are specific enough that `require_tech_in_description` was not enabled.

**Gotcha found here, fixed same day (see "Key Bugs We Hit" table below):** that same "Senior Full Stack Application Developer" posting explicitly requires "12+ years of professional software development experience" in its Required Technical Skills section and should have been dropped by the shared `_YOE_RE` 10+-years filter, but wasn't — the regex's optional qualifier group (`relevant|total|professional|work`) had to sit *immediately* before the literal word "experience", and this JD's actual phrasing inserts "software development" in between ("12+ years of **professional software development** experience"), so the pattern never matched at that position. Fixed by widening `_YOE_RE` to accept a bounded whitelist of filler words (e.g. `software`, `development`, `it`, `industry`, `technical`) between the qualifier and "experience" instead of requiring them adjacent — a global `matcher.py` regex change affecting all 275 companies, not a one-off fix for this onboarding.

**Registry/config changes:** `_PIPELINE_DATA` (`bookingcom`), `_IGNORES_KEYWORDS`, `_INLINE_DESCRIPTIONS`; `config.yaml` gained `bookingcom_search`; `seen_jobs_bookingcom.json` created as `[]`. Validated with `python src/run_all.py --validate --companies bookingcom` (ok) and a direct `run_company.run_company_pipeline("bookingcom", ...)` call using a temp seen-state path and a stub notifier (no real alerts sent, no tracked state advanced) — near-miss log showed correct `[title family]`/`[exclude]`/`[skill]` tags with no obvious false positives, and the one real match's tags and description were sane.

**Note on the working tree at the time of this addition:** other, unrelated onboarding work (Agoda, Airbnb, DoorDash, Priceline, Grab) was landing concurrently in this same repo from parallel sessions while this change was made, so `tests/test_company_registry_runner.py`'s exact registry-count assertion was a moving target — it was nudged to the count observed at the time but may need a final adjustment once all concurrent work is merged together.

## Genuine skip: Trivago (2026-09-13)

Germany-headquartered hotel-search metasearch company; investigated rather than assumed, per instructions. Careers site (`careers.trivago.com`) is WordPress (Divi theme) with a custom in-house plugin, `trivago-divi-job-positions-extension`, driving the job board — not a third-party ATS at all. The plugin's AJAX endpoint (`POST /wp-admin/admin-ajax.php`, `action=trivago_filter_open_positions`) returns a small JSON payload with `total`/`data[]` (`ID`, `permalink`, `post_title`, `job_categories`, `job_experience_levels`, `job_languages`, `job_locations`) — a genuine, easy-to-parse REST-ish API, no Playwright needed, and no keyword or location filter params exist on it at all (only `job_families`/`experience_levels`).

The problem is scale, not access: querying with `load_all_jobs=1` returns the company's **entire global open-position pool — 9 jobs total**, and every single one is located in **Düsseldorf, Germany** (trivago's HQ). There is no India location facet to check because there is no non-Düsseldorf location in the data at all — confirmed by paging the full unfiltered pool, not by trusting a broken/absent filter (the Micron/Verizon/Lowe's lesson). Third-party aggregators (Naukri showing "4334 Trivago jobs", LinkedIn showing "43-47 Trivago jobs in India") are the familiar aggregator-noise pattern already flagged for Intellect Design Arena/Temenos/CVS Health/Euroclear — junk/stale reposts and recruiter-agency listings referencing the brand name, not real reqs on trivago's own official pipeline. A company with 9 total open positions worldwide cannot plausibly have thousands of India listings on its own portal.

No fetcher, registry entry, config section, or `seen_jobs_trivago.json` created — same disposition as the other genuine skips in this table. If trivago's hiring picture changes (e.g. it opens a real India office/GCC), the AJAX endpoint and field shape documented above are ready to reuse directly.

No fetcher, registry entry, config section, or `seen_jobs_block.json` created. If Block opens an India office/GCC in the future, the board token (`block`) and API shape above are ready to reuse directly — it would be one of the simplest integrations in this repo (standard public Greenhouse REST API, inline descriptions, no bot protection observed).

No fetcher, registry entry, config section, or `seen_jobs_pinterest.json` created — same disposition as the other genuine skips in this table. If Pinterest opens a real India engineering presence, the board slug (`pinterest`) and standard Greenhouse `boards-api.greenhouse.io/v1/boards/{slug}/jobs?content=true` shape documented above are ready to reuse directly (copy `wellsfargo_fetcher.py`'s REST-JSON pattern, or any other Greenhouse-based fetcher already in this repo, e.g. Payoneer's).
